Clear direction — loss shrinks monotonically as the edge widens:

| edge | fills | final PnL |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | 164 | **−12,946** |
| 15 | 53 | −7,538 |
| 35 | 54 | **−3,655** |

**Caveat, and it matters:** all three ran simultaneously, so the `edge=2` quoter was sitting at the top of the book absorbing sweeps that would otherwise have hit the wider two. That flatters the wide quotes. The direction matches the theory, but these numbers aren't clean — a proper test runs them sequentially.

  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/emilygibbons/candidate/NOTES.md",
  "old_string": "### Environment note",
  "new_string": "### Why v1 of the quoter loses money\n\nFirst working quoter: fair = BBO mid, quote at fair +/- EdgeTicks, skew both quotes\nagainst inventory, cancel-replace only when the target price changes. Position\ncontrol worked immediately (held -9..+6 against a limit of 25, flattened itself\nrepeatedly). Profitability did not: about -270 over 15s, and -8,000 to -13,000 over\n95s.\n\n**First diagnosis, wrong-ish.** I had set `SkewTicks`(4) > `EdgeTicks`(2), so once\nthe position passed `MaxPos * Edge/Skew` (~12) the skew quoted *through* fair value\n— deliberately selling below fair to get flat. Real bug, and the `fill B 5 @ 650 /\nfill S 5 @ 650` pairs in the log are it. Fixed with a minimum-edge floor: skew may\nmake the flattening side more attractive but never past `MinEdgeTicks` of margin;\nthe discouraging side stays unbounded. Clearing inventory at a loss is the hedger's\njob, and it can do it in one trade instead of waiting to be lifted.\n\n**But the floor barely moved PnL**, which meant the real cause was elsewhere.\n\n**Actual cause: the sample market is built to run over resting quotes.**\n`sim/market.py:201` — the \"mover\" picks a target 20-60 points away every 2.5-4.5s\n(doubled for ~1/3 of moves, so up to 120), then sweeps with an order sized\n`1.2 x` everything resting in its path. `sim/market.py:142` — the background\nquoters rest 2-5 ticks off centre. So an edge of 2 puts us permanently at the top\nof the book: first in the queue for the casual flow we want, and first in the path\nof a 40-point sweep every three seconds.\n\nArithmetic: ~30 sweeps per 95s x 5 lots x ~40 points ~= 6,000. That is the observed\nloss, so the accounting is trustworthy and the strategy is simply mispriced. Fills\nwhere we bought at 537 and sold at 496 two hundred milliseconds later are this.\n\n**Evidence that edge width is the lever** (95s, sample market):\n\n| EdgeTicks | fills | final PnL |\n|-----------|-------|-----------|\n| 2         | 164   | -12,946   |\n| 15        | 53    | -7,538    |\n| 35        | 54    | -3,655    |\n\nCaveat on that table: the three ran **concurrently**, so the tight quoter absorbed\nsweeps that would otherwise have reached the wider two, which flatters the wide\nnumbers. The ordering matches the theory but needs sequential runs to be\nconclusive. Recording it as directional evidence, not a result.\n\n**What I am not going to do: tune EdgeTicks to 35.** The grading market is not this\nmarket (TASK.md), and 35 is fitted to this mover's step size. The generalisable\nstatement is *edge must scale with realised volatility* — so the quoter should\nmeasure how far the mid actually moves over a rolling window and set its edge from\nthat, which adapts to whatever the grading market does.\n\nTwo notes found while reading the sim:\n\n- `sim/market.py:179` repeats the changelog's false claim that `F` \"executes\n  atomically (fills in full or rejects), so there is no partial-execution handling\n  here\". Same myth in a third place. The sim uses `publish` rather than `request`\n  for order entry, so it never sees a reject and never notices.\n- The mover's target is clamped to [440, 760], so the sample market mean-reverts\n  inside a band. Anything that appears profitable by leaning on that band is\n  fitted to the sim and will not survive grading.\n\n### Environment note"

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:06:17
==============================================================================
Clean — only the sim stack running.

**Where the quoter stands:** the machinery is right and the pricing isn't. Position control, fill accounting, queue-preserving requoting, id discipline, rate limiting and clean shutdown all work. It loses money because a 2-tick edge is mispriced for a market that moves 40 points every three seconds, and I've now got the mechanism nailed down rather than guessed at.

The fix I'd make is **adaptive edge** — measure how far the mid actually moves over a rolling window and set the edge from that. That adapts to whatever the grading market does, whereas hardcoding 35 would be fitting to this sim's mover and the task says outright that grading uses a different market.

**But I'd do the hedger first**, for two reasons. It's a required deliverable that doesn't exist at all yet, and it's the seat the task cares most about — *"a large open position even for just a second is high risk."* It also changes how the quoter should be tuned: once something else is cutting inventory fast, the quoter can stop trying to flatten itself through skew and concentrate on never giving up margin.

Design is already settled by what we found: read every seat's fills from `ex.md.<FEED>.<sender>` rather than their self-reported status, since the taker proves a seat's own bookkeeping can be confidently wrong. Sum the three positions, and when the desk total breaches a threshold, cut it with `F` orders — handling partial fills properly, which the taker doesn't.

One thing worth building alongside it: a **benchmark script** that runs a given config against the sim for N seconds and reports final PnL and position, so we stop eyeballing log tails and can run the sequential comparisons the edge table needs. That's also exactly the "probes and test tooling" the deliverables ask you to include.

Shall I build the hedger next, or the benchmark harness first so we can measure the quoter properly before adding a third moving part?

**`strategy/quoter.go`** (modified, +33) — the strategy brain: keeps the book and position, decides prices, places and cancels orders, reports status. **The three untracked Go files** are what make it a program:

- **`exchange.go`** — everything about talking to the exchange: monotonic 8-char order ids that are never recycled, the self-imposed rate limit, reply parsing, and cancel-by-id.
- **`go.mod`** — module definition and the NATS client dependency.

**`tools/watch.sh`** — the readable market viewer, printing only genuine top-of-book changes plus trades.

[tool: Write]
{
  "file_path": "/Users/emilygibbons/candidate/hedger/hedger.py",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Desk hedger.\n\nKeeps the desk's *combined* position -- quoter + taker + hedger -- near zero.\nWhen the other seats accumulate exposure, this reduces it by crossing the spread.\nIt is the seat that is allowed to pay for speed: a small position held for a while\nis cheap, a large one held for a second is not.\n\nTwo decisions worth stating up front, both forced by what we found in the exchange\n(see NOTES.md):\n\n1. Positions are derived from the exchange's own fill feed\n   (`ex.md.<FEED>.<sender>`), never from a seat's self-reported\n   `strat.<sender>.status`. The shipped taker reports `pos=0` while being unable to\n   trade at all, and books sells as buys once it can. A hedger that trusts its\n   siblings' bookkeeping inherits their bugs; the exchange cannot be wrong about\n   what it matched.\n\n2. `F` (fill-and-kill) orders fill *partially*, despite what CHANGELOG.md v2.3 and\n   sim/market.py both claim. Every send therefore reads the traded volume back off\n   the reply and the remainder is re-hedged on the next tick.\n\nConfig (env):\n  NATS_URL         default nats://127.0.0.1:4222\n  HEDGER_SENDER    our sender tag               (default HEDGE001)\n  SENDER           the quoter's sender          (default QUOTE001)\n  TAKER_SENDER     the taker's sender           (default PYTKR001)\n  HEDGER_FEED      contract to hedge            (default AAH6, or TAKER_FEED)\n  HEDGE_THRESH     desk position that triggers a hedge   (default 5)\n  HEDGE_CLIP       max size per hedge order              (default 25)\n  HEDGE_SLIP       ticks through the touch we will pay   (default 10)\n  HEDGE_INTERVAL   seconds between checks                (default 0.05)\n  HEDGE_MAX_TPS    self-imposed request rate cap         (default 20)\n\"\"\"\nimport asyncio\nimport os\nimport time\n\nimport nats\n\nNATS_URL = os.environ.get(\"NATS_URL\", \"nats://127.0.0.1:4222\")\nSENDER = os.environ.get(\"HEDGER_SENDER\", \"HEDGE001\")\nFEED = os.environ.get(\"HEDGER_FEED\") or os.environ.get(\"TAKER_FEED\", \"AAH6\")\nSEATS = {\n    \"quoter\": os.environ.get(\"SENDER\", \"QUOTE001\"),\n    \"taker\": os.environ.get(\"TAKER_SENDER\", \"PYTKR001\"),\n    \"hedger\": SENDER,\n}\nTHRESH = int(os.environ.get(\"HEDGE_THRESH\", \"5\"))\nCLIP = int(os.environ.get(\"HEDGE_CLIP\", \"25\"))\nSLIP = int(os.environ.get(\"HEDGE_SLIP\", \"10\"))\nINTERVAL = float(os.environ.get(\"HEDGE_INTERVAL\", \"0.05\"))\nMAX_TPS = int(os.environ.get(\"HEDGE_MAX_TPS\", \"20\"))\n\nBASE36 = \"0123456789abcdefghijklmnopqrstuvwxyz\"\n\n\ndef base36(n):\n    \"\"\"8-char order id. Ids are consumed permanently per sender -- cancelling does\n    not free them (reject 203) -- so this only ever counts up. Seeding from the\n    wall clock means a container restart resumes above everything the last run\n    burned; ms since epoch (~1.8e12) fits inside 36^8 (~2.8e12).\"\"\"\n    out = []\n    for _ in range(8):\n        out.append(BASE36[n % 36])\n        n //= 36\n    return \"\".join(reversed(out))\n\n\ndef flip(side):\n    return \"S\" if side == \"B\" else \"B\"\n\n\nclass Hedger:\n    def __init__(self, nc):\n        self.nc = nc\n        self.positions = {name: 0 for name in SEATS}\n        # Volume we have traded but not yet seen echoed back on our own market-data\n        # feed. Without this the desk looks unhedged for as long as the round trip\n        # takes and we fire the same hedge repeatedly.\n        self.inflight = 0\n        self.best_bid = None\n        self.best_ask = None\n        self.oid = int(time.time() * 1000)\n        self.hedges = 0\n        self.traded = 0\n        self.cash = 0\n        self.last_send = 0.0\n        self.min_gap = 1.0 / MAX_TPS if MAX_TPS > 0 else 0.0\n\n    # ---- market data ------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n    async def on_bbo(self, msg):\n        f = msg.data.decode().split()\n        if len(f) < 6:\n            return\n        self.best_bid = None if f[2] == \"-\" else int(f[2])\n        self.best_ask = None if f[4] == \"-\" else int(f[4])\n\n    def make_md_handler(self, seat, sender):\n        \"\"\"One handler per seat, reading that seat's own fills off the exchange.\"\"\"\n        prefix = sender + \":\"\n\n        async def handler(msg):\n            f = msg.data.decode().split()\n            # <ts> E <incoming:17> <resting:17> <volume> <price> <matchid> <B|S>\n            # Only E is an execution; T reports the same trade again and would\n            # double-count it.\n            if len(f) < 8 or f[1] != \"E\":\n                return\n            incoming_is_ours = f[2].startswith(prefix)\n            resting_is_ours = f[3].startswith(prefix)\n            if incoming_is_ours and resting_is_ours:\n                return  # seat traded with itself: no net position change\n            if incoming_is_ours:\n                side = f[7]          # the trailing field is the aggressor's side\n            elif resting_is_ours:\n                side = flip(f[7])    # we were resting, so we took the other side\n            else:\n                return\n            vol, px = int(f[4]), int(f[5])\n            signed = vol if side == \"B\" else -vol\n            self.positions[seat] += signed\n            if seat == \"hedger\":\n                self.cash -= signed * px\n                # This fill is now confirmed, so it is no longer in flight.\n                if self.inflight > 0:\n                    self.inflight = max(0, self.inflight - vol)\n                elif self.inflight < 0:\n                    self.inflight = min(0, self.inflight + vol)\n\n        return handler\n\n    # ---- hedging ----------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n    def desk(self):\n        \"\"\"Desk exposure including anything we have fired but not yet seen back.\"\"\"\n        return sum(self.positions.values()) + self.inflight\n\n    async def request(self, msg):\n        gap = self.min_gap - (time.monotonic() - self.last_send)\n        if gap > 0:\n            # Exceeding the exchange's max_tps disconnects the sender outright\n            # (changelog v2.3) rather than rejecting, so we throttle ourselves.\n            await asyncio.sleep(gap)\n        self.last_send = time.monotonic()\n        try:\n            reply = await self.nc.request(f\"ex.req.{SENDER}\", msg.encode(), timeout=1.0)\n        except Exception as e:\n            print(f\"[hedger] request failed: {e}\", flush=True)\n            return None\n        return reply.data.decode().split()\n\n    async def hedge(self):\n        exposure = self.desk()\n        if abs(exposure) < THRESH:\n            return\n        # Long the desk -> sell. Short -> buy.\n        side = \"S\" if exposure > 0 else \"B\"\n        size = min(abs(exposure), CLIP)\n\n        # Price is a slippage limit, not the expected fill price: the exchange\n        # matches against resting orders at *their* prices, so this sweeps the book\n        # from the touch and refuses to pay more than SLIP ticks through it.\n        if side == \"S\":\n            if self.best_bid is None:\n                return\n            price = self.best_bid - SLIP\n        else:\n            if self.best_ask is None:\n                return\n            price = self.best_ask + SLIP\n\n        self.oid += 1\n        order = f\"{SENDER} A {FEED} {base36(self.oid)} {side} {size} {price} F\"\n        parts = await self.request(order)\n        if not parts or len(parts) < 2:\n            return\n        if parts[1] != \"Y\":\n            code = parts[2] if len(parts) > 2 else \"?\"\n            print(f\"[hedger] rejected {code}: {' '.join(parts[3:])}\", flush=True)\n            return\n\n        # F fills partially. The reply's count is what actually traded; whatever is\n        # left over stays on the books and gets re-hedged on the next tick.\n        filled = int(parts[2]) if len(parts) > 2 else 0\n        if filled:\n            self.inflight += filled if side == \"B\" else -filled\n            self.hedges += 1\n            self.traded += filled\n            print(f\"[hedger] {side} {filled}/{size} @<={price} \"\n                  f\"desk {exposure} -> {self.desk()}\", flush=True)\n\n    async def hedge_loop(self):\n        while True:\n            await asyncio.sleep(INTERVAL)\n            try:\n                await self.hedge()\n            except Exception as e:  # never let one bad tick stop the risk control\n                print(f\"[hedger] hedge error: {e}\", flush=True)\n\n    # ---- reporting --------------------------------------------------------- #\n\n    async def report_loop(self):\n        while True:\n            await asyncio.sleep(1.0)\n            pos = \" \".join(f\"{k}={v}\" for k, v in self.positions.items())\n            s = (f\"desk={self.desk()} {pos} inflight={self.inflight} \"\n                 f\"hedges={self.hedges} traded={self.traded} cash={self.cash}\")\n            print(f\"[hedger] {s}\", flush=True)\n            await self.nc.publish(f\"strat.{SENDER}.status\", s.encode())\n\n\nasync def main():\n    nc = await nats.connect(NATS_URL, max_reconnect_attempts=-1)\n    h = Hedger(nc)\n    await nc.subscribe(f\"ex.bbo.{FEED}\", cb=h.on_bbo)\n    for seat, sender in SEATS.items():\n        await nc.subscribe(f\"ex.md.{FEED}.{sender}\", cb=h.make_md_handler(seat, sender))\n    print(f\"[hedger] {SENDER} watching {sorted(SEATS.values())} on {FEED} \"\n          f\"thresh={THRESH} clip={CLIP} slip={SLIP}\", flush=True)\n    await asyncio.gather(h.hedge_loop(), h.report_loop())\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    asyncio.run(main())\n"
}

Now it's unambiguous:

```
ex.md.AAH6.MOVER001   E AGGR0003:aggsell3 MOVER001:18038059 6 574 ... S   ← passive party gets E
ex.md.AAH6.AGGR0003   T AGGR0003:aggsell3 MOVER001:18038059 6 574 ... S   ← aggressor gets T
```

**`E` goes to whoever was resting; `T` goes to whoever crossed.** Same fields, different recipient. My earlier "the resting side got nothing" test was misleading — that order simply never traded, since the aggressor matched a better-priced bid first.

So my comment in the quoter that "T reports the same trade again and would double-count" was wrong, and both seats need to handle both. Fixing:

[tool: Edit]
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/emilygibbons/candidate/NOTES.md",
  "old_string": "### Environment note",
  "new_string": "### `E` vs `T`: who gets told about a fill (undocumented)\n\nPROTOCOL.md describes `E` as \"an execution\" and `T` as reporting \"a trade --\nuseful for watching trade flow\", which reads like `T` is a duplicate of `E` for\nobservers. It is not. Traced by subscribing to every `ex.md.AAH6.*` subject through\na single match:\n\n```\nex.md.AAH6.MOVER001   E AGGR0003:aggsell3 MOVER001:18038059 6 574 90585 S\nex.md.AAH6.AGGR0003   T AGGR0003:aggsell3 MOVER001:18038059 6 574 90585 S\n```\n\n**A seat gets `E` on its own subject when it was the resting side of a match, and\n`T` when it was the aggressor.** Same fields, different recipient, never both for\none fill. So:\n\n- Deriving your position from `ex.md.<FEED>.<you>` requires handling **both**.\n- Filtering to `E` only (which both of my seats did at first, on my wrong\n  assumption that `T` was a duplicate) silently drops every fill made by crossing\n  the spread — i.e. *every* fill the hedger makes. It reported `hedger=0` while\n  actually trading.\n- One `F` order also arrives as **several** messages (a sell of 10 came back as\n  3@757, 5@756, 2@756). PROTOCOL's \"accumulate them\" is real.\n\nBlind alley worth recording: an intermediate probe showed a resting order receiving\nno fill message at all, which looked like it contradicted the above. It did not —\nthat order simply never traded, because the aggressor matched a better-priced order\nfirst. I nearly drew a conclusion from it.\n\n### The hedger\n\n`hedger/hedger.py`. Watches all three seats' fills on `ex.md.<FEED>.<sender>`,\nsums them into a desk position, and crosses the spread with `F` orders when the\ntotal breaches `HEDGE_THRESH`.\n\nDesign points:\n\n- **Positions come from the exchange, never from `strat.<sender>.status`.** The\n  shipped taker reports `pos=0` while being incapable of trading; a hedger that\n  trusted it would inherit that. Verified the principle on our own output too:\n  recomputing HEDGE001's position independently from the raw feed gave -10 against\n  a self-reported -10.\n- **Partial fills are read back off the reply.** `F` fills partially, so the send\n  applies `Y <n>`, not the requested size, and the remainder is re-hedged next tick.\n- **In-flight bridge.** Between firing a hedge and seeing the fill echoed back, the\n  desk would otherwise look unhedged and we would fire the same hedge again. A\n  signed `inflight` counter covers the gap.\n- **Price is a slippage limit, not a target.** The exchange matches at the resting\n  orders' prices, so `best_bid - HEDGE_SLIP` sweeps from the touch while refusing\n  to pay more than `SLIP` ticks through it.\n\n**Bug found and measured.** The first version retired in-flight volume by magnitude\ntoward zero rather than by signed amount, which corrupted the count as soon as\nhedges in both directions were outstanding. It fired 629 hedges and 15,225 lots in\n60 seconds while the desk swung to +/-55. One-line fix (`inflight -= signed`), same\n60-second workload:\n\n| | before | after |\n|---|---|---|\n| max abs desk position | 55 | **5** |\n| mean abs desk position | 23.55 | **0.32** |\n| samples at/over threshold | 63% | **2%** |\n| hedges fired | 629 | 14 |\n| lots traded | 15,225 | 73 |\n\nThe quoter's inventory is now mirrored almost exactly by the hedger (quoter +25,\nhedger -25, desk 0), which is what Job 2 asks for.\n\nI also suspected the hedger was crossing into the desk's own quoter, which would be\nchurn for nothing. Checked before acting: **zero** of its fills matched QUOTE001.\nThe oscillation was entirely the in-flight bug.\n\nStill open on the hedger:\n\n- Only exercised against a dead taker (`taker=0` throughout), so the three-seat\n  case is untested until the taker's order subject is fixed.\n- Hedging costs spread by design — that is the price of the risk control — but the\n  hedger's own PnL is not separated out yet, so the cost is unquantified.\n- `HEDGE_THRESH=5` / `CLIP=25` / `SLIP=10` are unjustified starting values, not\n  tuned.\n\n### Environment note"
}

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:24:31
==============================================================================
Hedger's built and working. Two things came out of it worth keeping.

**A protocol discovery that isn't in the docs.** PROTOCOL.md describes `T` as "useful for watching trade flow", which reads like it's a duplicate of `E` for observers. It isn't: **you get `E` when you were the resting side of a match, `T` when you crossed.** Different recipients, never both for one fill. Both my seats filtered for `E` only, on my wrong assumption — which silently discarded *every* fill the hedger made, since it only ever aggresses. It was reporting `hedger=0` while actively trading.

**A bug of mine, with the measurement.** The in-flight counter retired volume by magnitude rather than by signed amount, which corrupted as soon as hedges in both directions were outstanding. Same 60-second workload, one-line fix:

| | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| max abs desk position | 55 | **5** |
| mean abs desk position | 23.6 | **0.32** |
| time at/over threshold | 63% | **2%** |
| hedges fired | 629 | 14 |
| lots traded | 15,225 | 73 |

The quoter's inventory now gets mirrored almost exactly — quoter +25, hedger −25, desk 0. And I checked the hedger's position independently by recomputing it from the raw exchange feed rather than trusting its own report: −10 both ways.

I also suspected it was crossing into our own quoter's resting orders. It wasn't — zero of its fills matched QUOTE001. The oscillation was entirely the in-flight bug, so that suspicion would have been a wasted "fix".

**What's left, roughly in the order I'd do it:**

1. **Run `./run.sh --sim --strategy` end to end.** That's the exact command grading uses and we've never run it — everything so far has been hand-launched containers. Cheapest way to catch a compose wiring problem.
2. **Fix the taker's three bugs.** The desk isn't really a three-seat desk until it trades, and the hedger's three-way path is untested with `taker=0` the whole time.
3. **The quoter's economics** — adaptive edge scaled to measured volatility, which is still the open profitability problem.
4. **`TRANSCRIPT.txt`**, which is a required deliverable you don't have yet.

Shall I do the end-to-end run next?

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:26:17
==============================================================================
Scoping to this hedger stretch (the min-edge floor is already in your commit; only the `E`/`T` fix is outstanding in the quoter):

**`hedger/hedger.py`** — new file, untracked. Created whole, then two corrections during testing:

1. `if len(f) < 8 or f[1] != "E"` → `f[1] not in ("E", "T")`. Without this it discarded every fill it made, because an aggressor is told via `T`, not `E`.
2. The in-flight bookkeeping:
```python
# before — decremented by magnitude toward zero
if self.inflight > 0:   self.inflight = max(0, self.inflight - vol)
elif self.inflight < 0: self.inflight = min(0, self.inflight + vol)
# after
self.inflight -= signed
```
That one line is the difference between max desk exposure 55 and 5.

**`hedger/Dockerfile`** — replaced the placeholder (which printed "put your hedger here" and slept) with `python:3.12-slim`, `pip install nats-py`, `CMD ["python", "-u", "hedger.py"]`. The `-u` matters: without unbuffered output, container logs arrive in delayed chunks and you can't watch the desk live.

**`strategy/quoter.go`** — the 11-line diff above: `case "E"` → `case "E", "T"`, plus replacing my incorrect comment claiming `T` would double-count. The quoter is built to stay passive so `E` is its normal path, but a limit order can still cross if the book moves between reading the BBO and the order landing — that fill would have been lost.

**`NOTES.md`** — +140 lines: the `E` vs `T` finding with the traced evidence and the blind alley that nearly misled me, and the hedger section with its design decisions, the before/after table, and the three open items.

**`CHANGELOG.md`** — still just your annotation. I haven't touched it.

Nothing else moved. The quoter's pricing logic, `exchange.go`, `main.go` and `tools/` are all untouched since your commit.

  "file_path": "/Users/emilygibbons/candidate/tools/export_transcript.py",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Export the Claude Code session log(s) to a readable TRANSCRIPT.txt.\n\nTASK.md asks for the complete agent transcript, and says a raw export is fine.\nClaude Code already writes every session to ~/.claude/projects/<escaped-cwd>/*.jsonl,\nso this renders that record rather than reconstructing anything after the fact --\nthe point is to show what actually happened, including the wrong turns.\n\nMultiple session files are concatenated in timestamp order, as TASK.md requires.\n\nUsage:\n    tools/export_transcript.py                    # -> TRANSCRIPT.txt\n    tools/export_transcript.py -o /tmp/t.txt      # somewhere else\n    tools/export_transcript.py --no-thinking      # drop the model's reasoning blocks\n    tools/export_transcript.py --max-result 2000  # truncate long tool output\n\"\"\"\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport pathlib\nimport sys\n\nRULE = \"=\" * 78\n\n\ndef project_dir(cwd):\n    \"\"\"Claude Code escapes the working directory path to name its log folder.\"\"\"\n    return pathlib.Path.home() / \".claude\" / \"projects\" / cwd.replace(\"/\", \"-\")\n\n\ndef blocks(content):\n    \"\"\"message.content is either a plain string or a list of typed blocks.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(content, str):\n        return [{\"type\": \"text\", \"text\": content}]\n    return content if isinstance(content, list) else []\n\n\ndef render_result(content, limit):\n    if isinstance(content, list):\n        parts = []\n        for b in content:\n            if isinstance(b, dict) and b.get(\"type\") == \"text\":\n                parts.append(b.get(\"text\", \"\"))\n            elif isinstance(b, dict):\n                parts.append(f\"[{b.get('type')}]\")\n            else:\n                parts.append(str(b))\n        text = \"\\n\".join(parts)\n    else:\n        text = str(content)\n    if limit and len(text) > limit:\n        text = text[:limit] + f\"\\n... [{len(text) - limit} more chars truncated]\"\n    return text\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"-o\", \"--out\", default=\"TRANSCRIPT.txt\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"-C\", \"--cwd\", default=str(pathlib.Path.cwd()),\n                    help=\"project directory the session ran in\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--no-thinking\", action=\"store_true\",\n                    help=\"omit the model's reasoning blocks\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--max-result\", type=int, default=0,\n                    help=\"truncate tool results to N chars (0 = complete, the default)\")\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    src = project_dir(args.cwd)\n    logs = sorted(src.glob(\"*.jsonl\"))\n    if not logs:\n        sys.exit(f\"no session logs found in {src}\")\n\n    entries = []\n    for log in logs:\n        with log.open() as fh:\n            for line in fh:\n                line = line.strip()\n                if not line:\n                    continue\n                try:\n                    entries.append(json.loads(line))\n                except json.JSONDecodeError:\n                    continue  # a session still being written can end mid-line\n    entries.sort(key=lambda e: e.get(\"timestamp\") or \"\")\n\n    out = []\n    turns = 0\n    for e in entries:\n        if e.get(\"type\") not in (\"user\", \"assistant\") or e.get(\"isMeta\"):\n            continue\n        msg = e.get(\"message\") or {}\n        role = msg.get(\"role\", e[\"type\"])\n        stamp = (e.get(\"timestamp\") or \"\")[:19].replace(\"T\", \" \")\n\n        rendered = []\n        for b in blocks(msg.get(\"content\")):\n            if not isinstance(b, dict):\n                continue\n            kind = b.get(\"type\")\n            if kind == \"text\" and b.get(\"text\", \"\").strip():\n                rendered.append(b[\"text\"].rstrip())\n            elif kind == \"thinking\" and not args.no_thinking:\n                if b.get(\"thinking\", \"\").strip():\n                    rendered.append(\"[thinking]\\n\" + b[\"thinking\"].rstrip())\n            elif kind == \"tool_use\":\n                params = json.dumps(b.get(\"input\", {}), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)\n                rendered.append(f\"[tool: {b.get('name')}]\\n{params}\")\n            elif kind == \"tool_result\":\n                body = render_result(b.get(\"content\", \"\"), args.max_result)\n                if body.strip():\n                    rendered.append(f\"[tool result]\\n{body.rstrip()}\")\n        if not rendered:\n            continue\n\n        turns += 1\n        label = \"USER\" if role == \"user\" else \"ASSISTANT\"\n        if e.get(\"isSidechain\"):\n            label += \" (subagent)\"\n        out.append(f\"{RULE}\\n{label}  {stamp}\\n{RULE}\\n\")\n        out.append(\"\\n\\n\".join(rendered) + \"\\n\\n\")\n\n    header = (\n        f\"Trading-desk task -- complete Claude Code transcript\\n\"\n        f\"Exported by tools/export_transcript.py from {len(logs)} session log(s).\\n\"\n        f\"{turns} messages. Tool calls and their results are included inline.\\n\\n\"\n    )\n    pathlib.Path(args.out).write_text(header + \"\".join(out))\n    print(f\"wrote {args.out}: {turns} messages from {len(logs)} log(s)\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"

  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"Export the Claude Code session log(s) to a readable TRANSCRIPT.txt.\n\nTASK.md asks for the complete agent transcript, and says a raw export is fine.\nClaude Code already writes every session to ~/.claude/projects/<escaped-cwd>/*.jsonl,\nso this renders that record rather than reconstructing anything after the fact --\nthe point is to show what actually happened, including the wrong turns.\n\nMultiple session files are concatenated in timestamp order, as TASK.md requires.\n\nUsage:\n    tools/export_transcript.py                    # -> TRANSCRIPT.txt\n    tools/export_transcript.py -o /tmp/t.txt      # somewhere else\n    tools/export_transcript.py --no-thinking      # drop the model's reasoning blocks\n    tools/export_transcript.py --max-result 2000  # truncate long tool output\n\"\"\"\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport pathlib\nimport sys\n\nRULE = \"=\" * 78\n\n\ndef project_dir(cwd):\n    \"\"\"Claude Code escapes the working directory path to name its log folder.\"\"\"\n    return pathlib.Path.home() / \".claude\" / \"projects\" / cwd.replace(\"/\", \"-\")\n\n\ndef blocks(content):\n    \"\"\"message.content is either a plain string or a list of typed blocks.\"\"\"\n    if isinstance(content, str):\n        return [{\"type\": \"text\", \"text\": content}]\n    return content if isinstance(content, list) else []\n\n\ndef render_result(content, limit):\n    if isinstance(content, list):\n        parts = []\n        for b in content:\n            if isinstance(b, dict) and b.get(\"type\") == \"text\":\n                parts.append(b.get(\"text\", \"\"))\n            elif isinstance(b, dict):\n                parts.append(f\"[{b.get('type')}]\")\n            else:\n                parts.append(str(b))\n        text = \"\\n\".join(parts)\n    else:\n        text = str(content)\n    if limit and len(text) > limit:\n        text = text[:limit] + f\"\\n... [{len(text) - limit} more chars truncated]\"\n    return text\n\n\ndef main():\n    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()\n    ap.add_argument(\"-o\", \"--out\", default=\"TRANSCRIPT.txt\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"-C\", \"--cwd\", default=str(pathlib.Path.cwd()),\n                    help=\"project directory the session ran in\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--no-thinking\", action=\"store_true\",\n                    help=\"omit the model's reasoning blocks\")\n    ap.add_argument(\"--max-result\", type=int, default=0,\n                    help=\"truncate tool results to N chars (0 = complete, the default)\")\n    args = ap.parse_args()\n\n    src = project_dir(args.cwd)\n    logs = sorted(src.glob(\"*.jsonl\"))\n    if not logs:\n        sys.exit(f\"no session logs found in {src}\")\n\n    entries = []\n    for log in logs:\n        with log.open() as fh:\n            for line in fh:\n                line = line.strip()\n                if not line:\n                    continue\n                try:\n                    entries.append(json.loads(line))\n                except json.JSONDecodeError:\n                    continue  # a session still being written can end mid-line\n    entries.sort(key=lambda e: e.get(\"timestamp\") or \"\")\n\n    out = []\n    turns = 0\n    for e in entries:\n        if e.get(\"type\") not in (\"user\", \"assistant\") or e.get(\"isMeta\"):\n            continue\n        msg = e.get(\"message\") or {}\n        role = msg.get(\"role\", e[\"type\"])\n        stamp = (e.get(\"timestamp\") or \"\")[:19].replace(\"T\", \" \")\n\n        rendered = []\n        for b in blocks(msg.get(\"content\")):\n            if not isinstance(b, dict):\n                continue\n            kind = b.get(\"type\")\n            if kind == \"text\" and b.get(\"text\", \"\").strip():\n                rendered.append(b[\"text\"].rstrip())\n            elif kind == \"thinking\" and not args.no_thinking:\n                if b.get(\"thinking\", \"\").strip():\n                    rendered.append(\"[thinking]\\n\" + b[\"thinking\"].rstrip())\n            elif kind == \"tool_use\":\n                params = json.dumps(b.get(\"input\", {}), indent=2, ensure_ascii=False)\n                rendered.append(f\"[tool: {b.get('name')}]\\n{params}\")\n            elif kind == \"tool_result\":\n                body = render_result(b.get(\"content\", \"\"), args.max_result)\n                if body.strip():\n                    rendered.append(f\"[tool result]\\n{body.rstrip()}\")\n        if not rendered:\n            continue\n\n        turns += 1\n        label = \"USER\" if role == \"user\" else \"ASSISTANT\"\n        if e.get(\"isSidechain\"):\n            label += \" (subagent)\"\n        out.append(f\"{RULE}\\n{label}  {stamp}\\n{RULE}\\n\")\n        out.append(\"\\n\\n\".join(rendered) + \"\\n\\n\")\n\n    header = (\n        f\"Trading-desk task -- complete Claude Code transcript\\n\"\n        f\"Exported by tools/export_transcript.py from {len(logs)} session log(s).\\n\"\n        f\"{turns} messages. Tool calls and their results are included inline.\\n\\n\"\n    )\n    pathlib.Path(args.out).write_text(header + \"\".join(out))\n    print(f\"wrote {args.out}: {turns} messages from {len(logs)} log(s)\")\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n"
}

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:31:50
==============================================================================
`TRANSCRIPT.txt` is ready — 266K, 274 messages, every tool call and result inline.

**How it's built.** [tools/export_transcript.py](tools/export_transcript.py) renders it from Claude Code's own session logs in `~/.claude/projects/`, rather than me writing a narrative after the fact. That matters for how this gets graded: the task says it would rather see how you convinced yourself something was true than a tidy write-up. So the wrong turns are all still in there — the F-order test that contaminated itself with 7-character order ids, the `X` cancel-many rabbit hole I abandoned, the self-trading theory I checked and disproved, and the two bugs I introduced and then measured my way out of. It handles multiple session files, concatenating in timestamp order as the task requires, so it keeps working if you continue in a new session tomorrow.

I spot-checked that the substantive moments survived the export — the taker's "No responders", the self-match check, the `inflight -= signed` fix, the `E`/`T` trace, and the desk exposure numbers are all present.

**One limitation, stated in the script's docstring so nobody's misled:** the model's internal reasoning is *not* recoverable. Those blocks are persisted with an empty body and only a cryptographic signature. I initially wrote a `--no-thinking` flag assuming reasoning was there, noticed the export contained zero of them, checked the raw log, and found 76 blocks all with empty bodies — so I removed the flag rather than leave one that quietly does nothing. What you get is prompts, replies, and the complete tool record.

**Keeping it current:** re-run `python3 tools/export_transcript.py` any time — it overwrites in place. I'll refresh it at each checkpoint from here on, and it's cheap enough that the final step before you package the submission should just be one more run.

Deliverables status: `strategy/` complete and building, `hedger/` complete and building, `NOTES.md` substantial, `TRANSCRIPT.txt` present. Still outstanding from the earlier list — the end-to-end `./run.sh --sim --strategy` check, the taker's three bugs, and the quoter's profitability.

Want me to take the end-to-end run next?

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:36:24

Short answer: **we're not.** The numbers you're reading are cash, not profit — and the quoter's PnL display is broken in exactly the situation this run ended in.

**What the market did.** From 00:33:16 onwards the book went completely empty and never came back. The quoter's `bid=- ask=-` means it had no quotes resting, and it stopped quoting because [quoter.go:198](strategy/quoter.go#L198) refuses to price without a two-sided market. Nothing traded for the last 50 seconds of a 70-second run.

That's a deadlock in the sample market, not in our code. [sim/market.py:224](sim/market.py#L224) has the mover skip its turn entirely if either side is missing, and the background quoters only requote when `fair_value` returns non-`None`, which needs at least one side. Since BBO messages only fire when the book changes, an empty book produces no messages, so nothing wakes anyone up. **Once the sample market is fully drained it stays dead.** Our hedger's sweeps are the likely drainer — `HEDGE_SLIP=10` means each hedge eats up to 10 ticks of depth.

**Why the PnL looks alarming.** `pnl=9065` equals `cash=9065` exactly. That's [quoter.go](strategy/quoter.go) only marking the position when `bidOK && askOK` — with no market it silently drops the position from the calculation and reports raw cash. It's reporting the profit of being short 14 lots while pretending those 14 lots don't exist.

Marking properly against the last traded price (~675, from `fill S 5 @ 675`):

| | cash | position | marked | real PnL |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| quoter | +9,065 | −14 | −9,450 | **≈ −385** |
| hedger | −5,147 | +8 | +5,400 | **≈ +253** |
| **desk** | | **−6** | | **≈ −132** |

About −130 over 70 seconds, most of it in a dead market. Roughly flat, not a disaster.

**Two real bugs this surfaced, though:**

1. **A reject code we hadn't seen:** `N 305 orderid not active`. Our catalogue has `206 orderid not used`; this is a different thing — the order existed but had already filled. [exchange.go](strategy/exchange.go) treats only `206` as benign, so a `305` is returned as an error and `reconcile` bails out before clearing its record of that order. The `C` message usually clears it first, which is why it recovered, but it's a race we're currently losing noisily.

2. **The hedger correctly refused to trade into an empty book** — it needs a best ask to buy and there wasn't one — which is right, but it means the desk sat at −6 for 50 seconds with no way to act. Worth knowing that's the failure mode.

Also worth noting: the taker sat at `pos=0 fills=0` the entire run, exactly as predicted. The desk is really two seats right now.

Want me to fix the PnL marking (carry the last known mid) and the `305` handling, and add both to the notes?

Confirmed precisely:

- **`206`** — the id was never used by this sender.
- **`305`** — the id *was* used, but the order is no longer on the book (cancelled already, or filled).

Both mean "not resting", which is exactly the state `cancel` wants. Fixing:

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:40:08
==============================================================================
Fixed and verified.

**What `305` actually means** — established by experiment rather than inferred from the wording, because `206` and `305` read as if they're the same thing:

```
C AAH6 NEVER001   (never used)       -> N 206 orderid not used
C AAH6 LIVE0001   (live)             -> Y 1
C AAH6 LIVE0001   (again)            -> N 305 orderid not active
C AAH6 FILL0001   (after it filled)  -> N 305 orderid not active
```

`206` is "this sender never used that id"; `305` is "the id was used, but the order isn't resting any more" — whether it was cancelled already or filled. Both describe the state a cancel is trying to *reach*, so both are success for us.

**The change** — [strategy/exchange.go](strategy/exchange.go), +18/−3: named both codes as constants and accepted them:

```go
if !r.ok && r.code != rejectOrderIDUnused && r.code != rejectOrderInactive {
    return fmt.Errorf("cancel %s rejected: %s", id, r)
}
```

**Verified against the live market.** Your run logged `cancel ... rejected: N 305` several times in 70 seconds; a 45-second run on the rebuilt image logged **zero** cancel rejections and no errors of any kind, across 177 fills. The race is now handled where it belongs rather than being papered over by a `C` message arriving in time.

The notes now carry the `305` row in the reject-code table plus the 206-vs-305 distinction and why it mattered, and `TRANSCRIPT.txt` is refreshed to 294 messages.

One thing that run reconfirms: it finished `pos=-15 cash=-3579 pnl=-12459` on the default `edge=2`, which is the mispricing we already measured, not a new problem.

Still outstanding from your list: the **PnL marking bug** — reporting raw cash as profit whenever the book is one-sided or empty, which is what made the last run look like +9,065 when it was roughly −385. Want that next?

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:45:40
==============================================================================
Done. **[strategy/quoter.go](strategy/quoter.go)**, +54: a `markPrice()` that values inventory by preference — live mid, else the side we'd actually have to trade against to get flat (bid if long, ask if short), else the last price seen. Staleness is *flagged* in the output (`pnl=-3302?`) rather than hidden, and it prints `n/a` if a position genuinely can't be valued. `lastMark` updates from both the BBO and from our own fills, so a real trade counts as a valid mark.

The principle behind it: a valuation that silently drops the position is worse than no valuation, because it reads as a profit. Your run reported **+9,065** while short 14 lots; the honest figure was about **−385**, and the desk including the hedger was roughly **−130** over 70 seconds.

**The notes now also carry the deadlock finding**, which I think is the more consequential one. Once the sample market's book empties it never recovers — [sim/market.py:224](sim/market.py#L224) has the mover skip its turn if either side is missing, the background quoters need a `fair_value` that requires at least one side, and BBO messages only fire on book changes. No changes, no messages, nobody wakes up. Our hedger is the likely drainer with `HEDGE_SLIP=10` sweeping up to 10 ticks of depth per hedge.

Two practical consequences: a quiet tail in a long local run may mean a dead market rather than a calm one, so statistics over that window are worthless — and our quoter can't restart it either, because seeding a one-sided market means taking a directional position, which is precisely what Job 1 tells you not to do. I've left that deliberate rather than "fixed".

`TRANSCRIPT.txt` refreshed to 311 messages.

What's left, unchanged in priority: **the taker's three bugs** (the desk is really two seats until then), **the quoter's profitability** via volatility-scaled edge, and a **benchmark harness** so we can measure sequentially instead of eyeballing log tails. The taker is the smallest and unblocks a genuine three-seat test of the hedger — shall I take that?

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:51:11
==============================================================================
The taker's cash is wrong by a factor of three: it reports **−22,820**, the exchange says **−7,731**.

That's a fourth bug, and it's the same family as the third. The reply tells you the *volume* that traded but not the *price*; `F` orders get price improvement (a buy fills at or below your limit), so booking `filled × limit_price` overstates every purchase and understates every sale. Both errors push the same way, which is why PnL reads −20,951 when reality is nearer −5,700. The only correct source is the fill feed:

55	    def mid(self):
56	        if self.best_bid is None or self.best_ask is None:

58	        return (self.best_bid + self.best_ask) / 2

147	    def mark(self):
148	        """Price used to value inventory.
149	
150	        Falls back to the side we would have to trade against, then to the last
151	        price seen. The old pnl() valued the position at zero whenever the mid was
152	        unavailable, which silently reported cash as profit -- and the sample
153	        market's book does go empty (see NOTES.md).
154	        """
155	        m = self.mid()
156	        if m is not None:
157	            return m
158	        if self.position > 0 and self.best_bid is not None:
159	            return float(self.best_bid)      # long: we would sell into the bid
160	        if self.position < 0 and self.best_ask is not None:
161	            return float(self.best_ask)      # short: we would buy from the ask
162	        return self.last_mark
163	
164	    def pnl(self):
165	        m = self.mid()
166	        return self.cash + (self.position * m if m is not None else 0)
167	
168	    async def publish_status(self):
169	        s = (f"pos={self.position} cash={self.cash:.0f} "
170	             f"pnl={self.pnl():.0f} fills={self.fills}")
171	        print(f"[taker] {s}", flush=True)
172	        await self.nc.publish(f"strat.{SENDER}.status", s.encode())
173	
174	    async def reporter(self):
175	        while True:
176	            await asyncio.sleep(1.0)
177	            await self.publish_status()

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/emilygibbons/candidate/NOTES.md",
  "old_string": "**Design consequence for the hedger:** do not trust any seat's self-reported\n`strat.<sender>.status`. Positions get derived from the exchange's own\n`ex.md.<FEED>.<sender>` fill feed, which is ground truth and cannot drift from\nwhatever a seat believes about itself. This is worth doing even with a correct\ntaker — a hedger that depends on its siblings' bookkeeping inherits their bugs.",
  "new_string": "**Design consequence for the hedger:** do not trust any seat's self-reported\n`strat.<sender>.status`. Positions get derived from the exchange's own\n`ex.md.<FEED>.<sender>` fill feed, which is ground truth and cannot drift from\nwhatever a seat believes about itself. This is worth doing even with a correct\ntaker — a hedger that depends on its siblings' bookkeeping inherits their bugs.\n\n#### Fixed — and there were five, not three\n\nThe three above, plus two more that only became visible once it could trade:\n\n4. **Fills were booked at the wrong price.** The reply gives the volume that\n   traded but *not* the price, and a marketable order fills at the resting orders'\n   prices, not at our limit — `F` buys fill at or below the limit, sells at or\n   above. Booking `filled * limit_price` therefore overstated every purchase and\n   understated every sale, both pushing the same way. Measured over 60s: it\n   reported `cash=-22820` where the exchange's own feed said `-7731`, making PnL\n   read -20,951 against a reality nearer -5,700. Fixed by booking fills in a new\n   `on_md` handler from `ex.md.<FEED>.<SENDER>` — the same ground-truth rule the\n   hedger already follows — and handling both `E` and `T` there.\n5. **`pnl()` valued the position at zero whenever the mid was unavailable**, the\n   identical bug the quoter had: it reported cash as profit. Now falls back to the\n   side we would have to trade against, then to the last price seen, and returns\n   `n/a` rather than guessing.\n\nAlso changed the default `TAKER_FEED` from `BTH6`, which is not a listed\ninstrument, to `AAH6`. ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 00:53:58
==============================================================================
Taker's fixed and verified. **It turned out to be five bugs, not three** — I went past the three you asked for because the extra two only became visible once it could actually trade, and leaving them would have made the desk's numbers wrong in a quieter way.

**The three:**
1. Orders went to bare `ex.req`, which matches nothing — now `ex.req.{SENDER}`. The bare `except: return` that hid this now logs.
2. `signed = qty if side == "B" else qty` — sells booked as buys. Now `-qty`.
3. `filled = CLIP` ignored the reply's actual traded volume. Now reads it.

**The two I found by measuring afterwards:**

4. **Fills were booked at the wrong price.** The reply gives volume but not price, and a marketable order fills at the *resting* orders' prices — buys at or below your limit, sells at or above. Booking `filled × limit_price` overstated every purchase and understated every sale, both errors pushing the same way. It reported `cash=-22820` where the exchange said `-7731`. Now booked in a new `on_md` handler from the fill feed, handling both `E` and `T`, the same ground-truth rule the hedger follows.
5. **`pnl()` valued the position at zero when the mid was missing** — the identical bug the quoter had, reporting cash as profit.

I also changed the default `TAKER_FEED` from `BTH6`, which isn't a listed instrument, to `AAH6`, and updated `taker/README.md`. An unconfigured local run was getting `202 bad feedcode` on every order.

**Verified against the exchange rather than against itself:**

```
taker reports : pos=-24 cash=2112 pnl=-8556 fills=398
exchange truth: pos=-24 cash=2112            fills=398
```

Exact on all three. Worth noting the shape of this: the position bug was loud and obvious, the cash bug was quiet and still looked plausible — a 3x error that no amount of staring at the logs would have caught without recomputing from the feed.

One consequence: the taker now genuinely trades and ended 45 seconds at **−24** position. That's real exposure the hedger has never had to handle, since it saw `taker=0` in every test so far. The three-seat path is finally testable.

Shall I run the full `./run.sh --sim --strategy` stack now and see whether the hedger keeps the desk flat with two seats generating inventory instead of one?

The exchange\ncontainer had been up for hours across many sim restarts. Order ids are consumed\npermanently per sender, the sim picks random starting ids for fixed senders\n(`MOVER001` etc.) and publishes fire-and-forget, so it never sees `203 re-used\norder id` — its seed orders were silently rejected and no market ever formed.\n`docker compose down` between runs fixes it. Grading starts from fresh containers,\nso this is a local-testing hazard, but a flat quiet log is worth two seconds of\nsuspicion before it is worth a conclusion.\n\n**2. `timeout` does not exist on macOS.** Several diagnostic checks of the form\n`timeout 5 nats sub ... 2>/dev/null` printed nothing, which I read as \"no market\ndata\". The command was not found; the error went to the suppressed stderr. I\nbriefly concluded the exchange had stopped publishing BBO and started theorising\nabout JetStream state. Re-checked with a backgrounded subscriber writing to a file\n— the method that had worked all along — and found **3,766 BBO messages in 8\nseconds**. The market had been fine the whole time. Suppressing stderr on a\ndiagnostic is how you turn a broken tool into a false finding.\n\n### Tooling written along the way"

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 01:05:05
==============================================================================
Full three-seat stack ran clean. **Zero rejects and zero errors** across all three seats.

**Risk control works.** Over 90 seconds, while absorbing a mean gross exposure of 31 lots (`|quoter| + |taker|`, peaking at 45):

```
max |desk| 5  |  mean |desk| 0.81  |  at/over threshold 1% of samples
```

**Profitability doesn't — and the hedger is now the biggest loser.** Marked at ~440:

| seat | position | PnL |
|---|---|---|
| quoter | −10 | −8,496 |
| taker | −27 | −4,895 |
| hedger | +37 | ~−9,600 |

That hedger number is the new information. It traded **1,311 lots across 215 hedges in 90 seconds**, and every one crosses the spread.

**This challenges a decision I made earlier and argued for confidently.** I capped the quoter's skew so it never gives up margin, on the grounds that clearing inventory is the hedger's job. But flattening via skew is *free* — the quoter still earns its edge, just biased — while flattening via the hedger *costs* the spread every time. I have the cheap flattener doing less work than the expensive one. `HEDGE_THRESH=5` compounds it: against mean gross exposure of 31, the hedger reacts almost continuously. The task's own risk model says a small position held a while is cheap and a large one is not — which argues for a *higher* threshold. We're currently buying a mean desk position of 0.81 for roughly 9,600 per 90 seconds.

**Two things nearly fooled me, both now in the notes.** The first full-stack attempt showed all seats at `pos=0` for 100 seconds — a stale exchange had exhausted the sim's order-id space, and since the sim publishes fire-and-forget it never saw the `203` rejects. Needs `docker compose down` between local runs; grading starts fresh so it won't hit this.

The second was my own: **macOS has no `timeout` command.** My `timeout 5 nats sub ... 2>/dev/null` checks printed nothing because the command didn't exist and the error went to suppressed stderr. I read that as "the exchange stopped publishing BBO" and started theorising about JetStream. Re-checking properly found 3,766 BBO messages in 8 seconds — the market had been fine throughout.

Next I'd sweep `HEDGE_THRESH` and let the quoter's skew carry more of the flattening, measuring max desk exposure *and* PnL rather than assuming tighter is better. That needs the benchmark harness to be worth anything. Want me to build the harness and run the sweep?

Treats unset and empty the same, so a compose\n    pass-through like `HEDGE_THRESH: ${HEDGE_THRESH:-}` falls back to the default\n    rather than crashing on int(\"\").\"\"\"\n    raw = os.environ.get(key, \"\")\n    if raw == \"\":\n        return default\n    try:\n        return cast(raw)\n    except ValueError:\n        print(f\"[hedger] bad {key}={raw!r}; using {default}\", flush=True)\n        return default\n\n\nTHRESH = env(\"HEDGE_THRESH\", 5, int)\nCLIP = env(\"HEDGE_CLIP\", 25, int)\nSLIP = env(\"HEDGE_SLIP\", 10, int)\nINTERVAL = env(\"HEDGE_INTERVAL\", 0.05, float)\nMAX_TPS = env(\"HEDGE_MAX_TPS\", 20, int)"

  "new_string": "    def mark(self):\n        \"\"\"Price used to value our own inventory. Falls back to the side we would\n        have to trade against, then to the last price seen -- never valuing a live\n        position at zero, which would report cash as profit.\"\"\"\n        if self.best_bid is not None and self.best_ask is not None:\n            return (self.best_bid + self.best_ask) / 2\n        own = self.positions[\"hedger\"]\n        if own > 0 and self.best_bid is not None:\n            return float(self.best_bid)\n        if own < 0 and self.best_ask is not None:\n            return float(self.best_ask)\n        return self.last_mark\n\n    def pnl(self):\n        \"\"\"Our own mark-to-market. This is the *cost of the risk control*: the\n        hedger crosses the spread every time, so it is expected to be negative.\n        It is worth measuring precisely because it is the price paid for the flat\n        desk position, and the two must be traded off against each other.\"\"\"\n        m = self.mark()\n        if m is None:\n            return None\n        return self.cash + self.positions[\"hedger\"] * m\n\n    async def report_loop(self):\n        while True:\n            await asyncio.sleep(1.0)\n            pos = \" \".join(f\"{k}={v}\" for k, v in self.positions.items())\n            p = self.pnl()\n            s = (f\"desk={self.desk()} {pos} inflight={self.inflight} \"\n                 f\"hedges={self.hedges} traded={self.traded} cash={self.cash} \"\n                 f\"pnl={'n/a' if p is None else format(p, '.0f')}\")"

[tool: Write]
{
  "file_path": "/Users/emilygibbons/candidate/tools/bench.sh",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Run the full desk against the sample market for a fixed window and report the\n# two numbers that matter together: how much risk the desk carried, and what it\n# cost. Tuning either one alone is how you end up with a perfectly flat desk that\n# loses money, or a profitable quoter that blows up.\n#\n#   tools/bench.sh                                   # defaults, 90s\n#   tools/bench.sh -d 120 HEDGE_THRESH=20\n#   tools/bench.sh -n \"wide\" QUOTER_EDGE=15 HEDGE_THRESH=20\n#\n# Any KEY=VALUE is exported for the run; docker-compose.yml passes the tunables\n# through to the seats. Each run starts from `docker compose down` because a\n# long-lived exchange silently breaks the sample market: order ids are consumed\n# permanently per sender, and the sim publishes fire-and-forget, so once it\n# collides with its own earlier ids its orders vanish without any error and no\n# market ever forms.\nset -euo pipefail\ncd \"$(dirname \"$0\")/..\"\n\nDURATION=90\nLABEL=\"\"\nwhile [ $# -gt 0 ]; do\n  case \"$1\" in\n    -d) DURATION=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n    -n) LABEL=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n    *=*) export \"${1?}\"; shift ;;\n    *) echo \"unknown argument: $1\" >&2; exit 1 ;;\n  esac\ndone\n[ -n \"$LABEL\" ] || LABEL=\"$(printenv | grep -E '^(QUOTER|HEDGE)_' | grep -v '=$' | paste -sd, - || echo defaults)\"\n[ -n \"$LABEL\" ] || LABEL=\"defaults\"\n\nlog() { printf '[bench] %s\\n' \"$*\" >&2; }\n\nlog \"run: $LABEL  (${DURATION}s)\"\ndocker compose down >/dev/null 2>&1 || true\ndocker compose --profile sim --profile strategy up -d --build >/dev/null 2>&1\n# Let the sim seed a book and the seats connect before the measurement window.\nsleep 15\nlog \"measuring...\"\nsleep \"$DURATION\"\n\ntmp=\"$(mktemp -d)\"\ntrap 'rm -rf \"$tmp\"' EXIT\ndocker compose logs --no-log-prefix --tail 2000 hedger   > \"$tmp/h.log\" 2>&1\ndocker compose logs --no-log-prefix --tail 2000 strategy > \"$tmp/q.log\" 2>&1\ndocker compose logs --no-log-prefix --tail 2000 taker    > \"$tmp/t.log\" 2>&1\ndocker compose down >/dev/null 2>&1 || true\n\n# Risk: the distribution of desk exposure, not just its endpoint. A desk that\n# averages zero by oscillating between -40 and +40 is not flat.\nrisk=\"$(grep -o 'desk=[-0-9]*' \"$tmp/h.log\" | cut -d= -f2 | awk -v d=\"$DURATION\" '\n  {n++; a=($1<0?-$1:$1); s+=a; if(a>mx)mx=a; if(a>=10)o10++; if(a>=25)o25++}\n  END{ if(!n){print \"no-data\"; exit}\n       printf \"%d %.2f %.0f %.0f\", mx, s/n, 100*o10/n, 100*o25/n }')\"\n\n# PnL: each seat marks its own inventory; \"n/a\" means it could not be valued.\npnl_of() { grep -o 'pnl=[-0-9]*' \"$1\" | tail -1 | cut -d= -f2; }\nqp=\"$(pnl_of \"$tmp/q.log\")\"; tp=\"$(pnl_of \"$tmp/t.log\")\"; hp=\"$(pnl_of \"$tmp/h.log\")\"\nchurn=\"$(grep -o 'traded=[0-9]*' \"$tmp/h.log\" | tail -1 | cut -d= -f2)\"\n\nread -r mx mean o10 o25 <<<\"$risk\"\ntotal=$(( ${qp:-0} + ${tp:-0} + ${hp:-0} ))\n\nprintf '\\n%s\\n' \"----------------------------------------------------------------\"\nprintf 'run            : %s (%ss)\\n' \"$LABEL\" \"$DURATION\"\nprintf 'RISK  max|desk|: %-6s mean|desk|: %-7s >=10: %s%%  >=25: %s%%\\n' \"$mx\" \"$mean\" \"$o10\" \"$o25\"\nprintf 'PNL   quoter   : %-8s taker: %-8s hedger: %-8s\\n' \"${qp:-n/a}\" \"${tp:-n/a}\" \"${hp:-n/a}\"\nprintf 'PNL   TOTAL    : %-8s   (hedger lots traded: %s)\\n' \"$total\" \"${churn:-0}\"\nprintf '%s\\n' \"----------------------------------------------------------------\"\n\n# Machine-readable, for collecting a sweep into one table.\nprintf 'CSV,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s,%s\\n' \\\n  \"$LABEL\" \"$DURATION\" \"$mx\" \"$mean\" \"$o10\" \"$o25\" \"${qp:-}\" \"${tp:-}\" \"${hp:-}\" \"$total\"\n"
}

==============================================================================
ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 03:42:29
==============================================================================
Harness built, sweep run — and it caught a flaw in my own method before it reached a conclusion.

**The first sweep was invalid.** `docker compose down` doesn't stop services belonging to a profile. It removed only nats and exchange, then failed to remove the network, while four seats kept running. The following `up` then *reused* any seat whose config was unchanged — so a repeat of the same setting silently continued from the previous run's positions and PnL. Fixed by passing profiles to every compose call plus `--force-recreate`. Corrected numbers differ by 2–3x and **reverse the conclusion**.

Clean results, 60s each:

| THRESH | max desk | mean desk | ≥10 | ≥25 | hedger lots | total PnL |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 2 | 10 | 0.97 | 1% | 0% | 1,147 | −15,627 |
| 5 | 12 | 1.19 | 1% | 0% | 683 | −6,404 |
| 5 | 6 | 0.96 | 0% | 0% | 617 | −5,101 |
| 15 | 15 | 4.82 | 12% | 0% | 222 | −7,615 |
| 15 | 18 | 5.15 | 16% | 0% | 152 | −7,438 |
| 30 | 28 | 11.43 | 55% | 5% | 75 | −5,961 |

**Keep `HEDGE_THRESH=5`.** Best risk profile measured — mean exposure ~1, never above 25 — at a PnL indistinguishable from the best. Threshold 30 reaches similar PnL while spending 55% of the session above 10 lots for no measurable gain.

**I retract my "raise the threshold" recommendation.** The reasoning behind it — skew flattens for free, hedging costs spread — is still sound in principle, but it rested on contaminated data and the clean measurements don't support it.

**Two things I got wrong that the harness corrected:**

The hedger isn't losing money — it makes **+2,500 to +4,000** in every run except the over-churning threshold 2. But I'd be careful calling that skill: it systematically takes the opposite side of whatever the desk accumulates, and the taker is a momentum strategy that loses. Fading a loser is profitable. That "profit" is really the taker's loss partly recovered, and it wouldn't survive fixing the taker.

And the taker, not the hedger, is the dominant loss *and* the dominant variance — **−5,053 to −8,328** every run, consistently worse than the quoter. It's a momentum strategy in a market that mean-reverts inside a band (`sim/market.py` clamps its target to [440, 760]). We own it, so its parameters are fair game.

Everything's in [tools/bench.sh](tools/bench.sh) with the compose pass-through wired up, notes updated with the retraction, transcript refreshed, no containers left running.

The obvious next target is the taker, since it's now the biggest single term in desk PnL — either retuning it or reducing its size. The quoter's adaptive edge is still open too.

==============================================================================
USER  2026-08-15 03:47:20
==============================================================================
what do you think is the best option?

==============================================================================
ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 03:47:52
==============================================================================
**The quoter's adaptive edge.** Three reasons it beats the alternatives:

**It's the actual deliverable.** Job 1 asks for a "low-risk, profitable quoting strategy." The quoter is the seat being graded on that, and right now it loses money in every measurement — at `edge=2`, which is the default that ships. Whatever else we do, grading will run that number.

**The fix is principled, not fitted.** "Set edge proportional to measured realised volatility" is a statement about market making in general: your spread has to cover how far the price moves while you're holding. It adapts to whatever the grading market does. Compare that to hardcoding `edge=35`, which is just the mover's step size in this particular sim, memorised.

**Retuning the taker now would be overfitting, and I'd argue against it.** Its −5,000 to −8,000 is real *here*, but it's a momentum strategy losing in a market that mean-reverts inside a hard band ([440, 760], set in `sim/market.py`). Momentum may well work in the grading market — the task says explicitly it isn't this market. Shrinking the taker because it loses against this specific mover is fitting to the sample, which is the same mistake I'd be avoiding by not hardcoding edge=35. Its losses are also mostly recovered by the hedger fading it, so the desk-level cost is smaller than the seat-level number suggests.

One caveat on my own recommendation: **the measurement is currently too noisy to prove an improvement.** The quoter's PnL varied from −1,066 to −4,246 across runs at *identical* settings — noise of ~3,000 on a signal we're hoping to move by a few thousand. So I'd do adaptive edge with 180-second runs and three repeats per setting, rather than the 60-second singles that just misled me twice. That costs maybe 20 minutes of wall time and is the difference between a result and a story.

If you want a hedge against running out of time: the smallest useful thing is to pick a better fixed default for `QUOTER_EDGE` from measured data and ship that, then build the adaptive version on top. That guarantees we don't submit with a default we know is wrong.

My suggestion is to start there — measure a proper edge curve with repeats, set the default from it, then make it adaptive. Want me to run that?

==============================================================================
USER  2026-08-15 03:48:28
==============================================================================
lets do the quoter. is it currently running every moment the market updates?

==============================================================================
ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 03:48:47
==============================================================================
Not every update — there are three separate throttles stacked, and I think they interact badly. From the code:

1. **`onBBO` dedupes** ([quoter.go:71](strategy/quoter.go#L71)) — identical top-of-book messages are dropped, and the feed republishes constantly, so most messages die here.
2. **The wake channel coalesces** ([quoter.go:57](strategy/quoter.go#L57)) — capacity 1, non-blocking send, so a burst of updates collapses into one pending requote.
3. **`requoteLoop` sleeps `MinRequote`** (50ms default) after every cycle, and a 1s ticker refreshes even when nothing moves.

But there's a fourth I'm less comfortable about: the client's **TPS limiter spaces *every request* 50ms apart** (`MaxTPS=20`). A cycle that repositions both sides is cancel+add × 2 = 4 requests = 200ms, plus the 50ms sleep. So a full two-sided requote may take ~250ms in a market that moves 40 points in a fraction of a second. Let me measure what's actually happening rather than infer it:

==============================================================================

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 03:50:21
==============================================================================
Measured over 30 seconds:

| | rate |
|---|---|
| BBO messages received | 4,622 (154/sec) |
| genuine top-of-book changes | 744 (24.8/sec) |
| our order actions (adds+cancels) | 246 (8.2/sec) |
| → quote repositions | ~4/sec |

So no — **we act on roughly 1 in 6 real market changes.** The layers do different jobs, and most of the filtering is happening in a place I'd defend: the dedupe kills 84% of messages that carry no new information, and `reconcile` then does nothing when the recomputed target price is unchanged, which is most of the time since prices round to integer ticks. Only acting when your own target moves is correct.

**What I'm less happy about is the latency when the target *does* move.** We're using 8.2 of a 20/sec budget, and `MinRequote` (20 cycles/sec) isn't binding either — neither throttle is actually saturated. But the TPS limiter enforces a **fixed 50ms gap between every request**, so a two-sided reposition (cancel + add, twice) costs ~200ms of forced waiting *despite being well under budget*. In a market where the mover sweeps the book in a single order, 200ms is an eternity to sit on stale quotes — and stale quotes in a fast market is precisely the adverse selection that's costing us.

That's a design mistake in [exchange.go](strategy/exchange.go): I implemented rate limiting as fixed spacing when it should be a **token bucket** — let a burst of 4 requests go immediately, refill at 20/sec, so the sustained rate is still protected but a reposition isn't artificially serialised. Same safety margin against the disconnect risk, much better reaction.

I'd do that before the adaptive edge, because edge width and reaction speed are the two halves of the same problem: a wider edge compensates for being slow, so tuning edge on top of an artificial 200ms delay would tune it to the wrong number. Fix the latency first, then measure the edge curve against a quoter that reacts properly.

Want me to do the token bucket, then run the edge sweep with the 180s × 3 repeats method?

  "new_string": "type midSample struct {\n\tat  time.Time\n\tmid float64\n}\n\n// pushMid records a mid and drops anything older than the volatility window.\n// Caller must hold q.mu.\nfunc (q *quoter) pushMid(mid float64) {\n\tnow := time.Now()\n\tq.mids = append(q.mids, midSample{at: now, mid: mid})\n\tcut := now.Add(-q.cfg.VolWindow)\n\ti := 0\n\tfor i < len(q.mids) && q.mids[i].at.Before(cut) {\n\t\ti++\n\t}\n\tif i > 0 {\n\t\tq.mids = append(q.mids[:0], q.mids[i:]...)\n\t}\n}\n\n// volatility is the standard deviation of the mid over the recent window, in\n// price units. Caller must hold q.mu.\n//\n// This is what makes the edge adaptive instead of fitted. A market maker's spread\n// has to cover how far the price moves while it is holding, and that distance is\n// a property of the market, not a constant: measured on the sample market, a fixed\n// edge of 2 lost 8,405 over 120s while 35 made 2,276 -- but 35 is simply this\n// sim's mover step size memorised, and TASK.md says grading uses a different\n// market. Caller must hold q.mu.\nfunc (q *quoter) edge() float64 {\n\te := q.cfg.EdgeTicks // floor: never quote tighter than this\n\tif q.cfg.EdgeVolMult > 0 {\n\t\tif v := q.cfg.EdgeVolMult * q.volatility(); v > e {\n\t\t\te = v\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif q.cfg.MaxEdgeTicks > 0 && e > q.cfg.MaxEdgeTicks {\n\t\te = q.cfg.MaxEdgeTicks // a burst of volatility should not price us off the book entirely\n\t}\n\treturn e\n}\n\n// target is the quote we want on one side; vol == 0 means \"do not quote\"."
}

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 04:19:22
==============================================================================
**The quoter is profitable.** Answering your original question first: it was reacting to about 1 in 6 genuine market changes — 24.8 top-of-book changes/sec, ~4 repositions/sec.

Most of that filtering was correct. What wasn't: the rate limiter used a **fixed 50ms gap between every request**, so repricing both sides serialised into ~200ms of sitting on stale quotes — while running at 8/sec against a 20/sec budget. Neither throttle was saturated; the limiter's *shape* was wrong. Replaced with a token bucket (burst 8, refill at MaxTPS): same protection against the disconnect risk, but a burst clears immediately. Acting on 23.5% of changes now, up from 16.5%, zero rejects.

Fixing that first mattered — a wider edge compensates for being slow, so tuning edge on top of a self-inflicted delay would have tuned it to the wrong number.

**Then the edge sweep**, 120s each. Quoter's own PnL: `edge=2` → **−8,405**, `10` → −3,874, `20` → −1,962, `35` → **+2,276**. Monotonic, and risk unaffected since the hedger keeps the desk flat regardless.

But 35 is this sim's mover step size memorised. So the edge is now `EdgeVolMult × stdev(mid over 2s)`, floored and capped. I calibrated the multiplier from data rather than guessing — measured rolling 2s stdev has median **7.5** — which showed my first guess of 1.5 gave an edge of ~11 and duly lost money. Multipliers of 3.0 and 4.5 both produced a profitable quoter (+5,659, +3,494).

**Shipping `QUOTER_EDGE_VOL=4.0`.** The multiplier is a risk appetite — "quote four sigma wide" — not a price level, so it's the part that transfers to a market with different absolute moves.

Confirmation at shipped defaults:

```
RISK  max|desk| 4   mean|desk| 0.69   >=10: 0%   >=25: 0%
PNL   quoter +1,074   taker -9,881   hedger -1,004
```

Both jobs demonstrably work: profitable two-sided quoting in 5 of 5 runs at multiplier ≥3, and a desk that never exceeded 4 lots of exposure.

Two caveats I'd keep visible. Quoter PnL among the profitable configs ranges +1,074 to +5,659, so the ranking *within* that group is noise — what's solid is the gap against the old fixed edge of 2. And **the taker is now the whole problem**: −9,881 at shipped defaults, once as bad as −28,860, dwarfing the quoter's profit. I've deliberately left it untuned, for the same reason I didn't hardcode edge=35.

+func newClient(nc *nats.Conn, sender, feed string, maxTPS, maxBurst int) *client {
 	return &client{
 		nc:     nc,
 		sender: sender,
 		feed:   feed,
 		subj:   "ex.req." + sender,
+		rate:   float64(maxTPS),
+		burst:  float64(maxBurst),
 		// Order ids are consumed permanently per sender -- cancelling does not
 		// free them (reject 203). Seeding from the wall clock means a container
 		// restart resumes above every id the previous run burned. Milliseconds
 		// since epoch is ~1.8e12, comfortably inside the 36^8 = 2.8e12 that fits

-		nextID:   uint64(time.Now().UnixMilli()),

+		nextID: uint64(time.Now().UnixMilli()),
+		tokens: float64(maxBurst), // start full: the first reposition should not wait
 	}
 }
 
+// reserve takes one token and returns how long the caller must wait for it.
+// Letting tokens go negative is deliberate: it queues concurrent callers instead
+// of letting them all decide independently that they may go now.
+func (c *client) reserve() time.Duration {
+	c.mu.Lock()
+	defer c.mu.Unlock()
+	if c.rate <= 0 {
+		return 0 // unlimited
+	}
+	now := time.Now()
+	if c.last.IsZero() {
+		c.last = now
+	}
+	c.tokens += now.Sub(c.last).Seconds() * c.rate
+	if c.tokens > c.burst {
+		c.tokens = c.burst
+	}
+	c.last = now
+	c.tokens--
+	if c.tokens >= 0 {
+		return 0
+	}
+	return time.Duration(-c.tokens / c.rate * float64(time.Second))
+}
+

==============================================================================
ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 04:24:09
==============================================================================
Two changes, both from the latency work.

**[strategy/exchange.go](strategy/exchange.go)** (+42 net) — replaced the rate limiter's mechanism:

- Fields `interval time.Duration` → `tokens, burst, rate float64` (`last` kept, now meaning "when the bucket was last refilled").
- New `reserve()` method: refills by elapsed time, takes one token, returns how long to wait. Letting `tokens` go negative is deliberate — it queues concurrent callers rather than letting each independently conclude it may go now.
- `newClient` gained a `maxBurst` parameter and starts the bucket **full**, so the first reposition never waits.
- `request()` lost the lock-sleep-unlock block, now just `if wait := c.reserve(); wait > 0 { time.Sleep(wait) }`.

**[strategy/main.go](strategy/main.go)** (+26 net) — config only, no logic:

- `EdgeTicks` comment changed from "half-spread we try to earn" to **"floor on the half-spread"** — its meaning changed, which is the one thing here that could surprise someone reading old code.
- Three new fields for the adaptive edge: `EdgeVolMult` (default 4.0), `MaxEdgeTicks` (120), `VolWindow` (2000ms).
- `MaxBurst` (default 8 — two full two-sided repositions back-to-back).
- `MaxTPS`'s comment now says *sustained* rate cap, since burst is separate.

Setting `QUOTER_EDGE_VOL=0` restores a fixed edge of `QUOTER_EDGE`, which is how I ran the fixed-edge sweep — the old behaviour is still reachable.

The actual volatility calculation isn't in either of these files — it's in [strategy/quoter.go](strategy/quoter.go) (`pushMid`, `volatility`, `edge`), which is also modified. `main.go` only supplies the parameters.

One thing I should check while we're looking at Go formatting — that struct literal looks misaligned in the diff:

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 04:28:34
==============================================================================
Confirmed — three structural defects, all market-independent:

**1. It never dedupes the BBO feed.** [taker.py:95](taker/taker.py#L95) appends to `mids` on *every message*. We measured 154 messages/sec against 24.8 genuine top-of-book changes/sec, so ~84% of that deque is duplicate prices. `TAKER_LAG=5` is documented as "BBO updates back the move is measured over" — in reality it's ~32ms of wall time, six times shorter than intended. The momentum signal is measuring noise.

**2. No cooldown.** `maybe_trade` runs on every message and keeps firing while the condition holds — one genuine move fires a burst of orders. That's why it racked up ~400 fills in 45 seconds.

**3. No rate limiting at all**, unlike the other two seats. The changelog says exceeding `max_tps` *disconnects* the sender — a session-ending risk for a desk seat.

Fixing those three, without touching `THRESH` or `LAG`:
