The\n   momentum signal was measuring noise over a window six times shorter than\n   intended. Now records only distinct tops, and the docs say so.\n2. **No cooldown.** `maybe_trade()` ran on every message and kept firing for as\n   long as the signal held, turning one price move into a burst of orders.\n3. **No rate limiting of any kind** — the only seat without it. Exceeding `max_tps`\n   *disconnects* the sender (changelog v2.3), which is session-ending for a desk\n   seat. `TAKER_COOLDOWN` (default 0.5s) now serves as both.\n\n**Measured effect on behaviour — large.** Same isolated 45s test, before and after:\n\n| | before | after |\n|---|---|---|\n| fill messages | 398 | **10** |\n| lots traded | 966 | **26** |\n| PnL (that run) | -8,556 | -317 |\n\nIt was trading roughly forty times more often than its own parameters intended.\n\n**Measured effect on desk PnL — none I can demonstrate.** Two full-desk runs after\nthe fix gave taker PnL of -13,248 and -4,960, against a -9,881 baseline. That\nstraddles the baseline, and two runs of *identical* configuration differing by\n8,300 is the real finding: this seat's PnL variance is larger than any effect I\nhave been able to produce in it.\n\nThe mechanism is consistent, though: it now takes far fewer trades but holds larger\ndirectional positions for longer, so the loss per lot goes up as the lot count goes\ndown. Holding 30 lots through one of this market's 300-point excursions is ~9,000\nregardless of how few orders it took to get there.\n\n**Conclusion: the remaining loss is the strategy, not the implementation.** Momentum\nin a market whose target mean-reverts inside a hard band (`sim/market.py` clamps to\n[440, 760]) loses by construction. The implementation is now correct and honest —\nit measures what it claims to measure and reports what actually happened.\n\nI am not tuning it further. `TAKER_MAX_POS` is the one defensible knob if we want\nto bound its damage, because capping worst-case exposure does not require believing\nanything about direction — but the current 30 is already fully absorbed by the\nhedger, so it is a PnL question rather than a desk-risk question, and any value I\npicked would be fitted to a market that is explicitly not the graded one.\n\n### Two ways this nearly fooled me"
}

==============================================================================
ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 04:36:40
==============================================================================
Taker's structural defects are fixed, and I want to be straight about what that bought.

**Behaviour: fixed, and the effect was large.** It was trading ~40× more often than its own parameters intended — 398 fills where the design implies ~10. The `LAG=5` window was spanning 32ms of duplicate quotes instead of five real price moves, and with no cooldown a single move fired a burst. It now also has a rate limit, which it was the only seat to lack — and that one is a genuine safety fix, since exceeding `max_tps` disconnects the sender outright rather than rejecting.

**Desk PnL: no improvement I can demonstrate.** Two full-desk runs gave −13,248 and −4,960 against a −9,881 baseline. That straddles it — and two runs of *identical* config differing by 8,300 is really the finding here: this seat's variance is larger than any effect I've managed to produce in it.

The mechanism is at least coherent. It now takes far fewer trades but holds bigger directional positions for longer, so loss-per-lot rises as lot count falls. Holding 30 lots through one of this market's 300-point excursions costs ~9,000 no matter how few orders got you there.

**So the remaining loss is the strategy, not the implementation.** Momentum in a market whose target mean-reverts inside a hard band ([440, 760], set in `sim/market.py`) loses by construction. I've stopped there rather than tuning `THRESH`/`LAG`, for the reason I gave before — those numbers would be fitted to a market the task says isn't the graded one.

The one knob I'd consider is `TAKER_MAX_POS`, since capping worst-case exposure doesn't require believing anything about direction. But the hedger already absorbs its position completely — desk exposure never exceeded 7 in these runs — so it's a PnL question, not a risk question, and any value I picked would be fitted to this sim.

Where the desk stands: quoter profitable, desk flat, taker correct but losing on its own merits. The open items are the noise problem (single 120s runs can't resolve differences under a few thousand) and whether you want to take a view on the taker's sizing — which is a judgement call about the strategy, and genuinely yours rather than mine.

They are encoded as tests because\nthe desk's correctness rests on them and the grading exchange may not be the same\nbuild: if one of these starts failing, a seat is silently wrong rather than loudly\nbroken.\n\nRuns against a live exchange. Needs no sample market -- it makes its own trades on\nan empty book, so every effect is caused by the test.\n\n    docker compose up -d\n    tests/run_tests.sh          # or, directly, inside a container with nats-py:\n    NATS_URL=nats://localhost:4222 python3 tests/test_protocol.py\n\"\"\"\nimport asyncio\nimport os\nimport random\nimport sys\n\nimport nats\n\nNATS_URL = os.environ.get(\"NATS_URL\", \"nats://127.0.0.1:4222\")\nFEED = os.environ.get(\"TEST_FEED\", \"AAH6\")\n# Order ids are consumed permanently per sender, so every run needs fresh senders\n# or it fails with 203 on the second execution.\nRUN = f\"{random.randint(0, 99999):05d}\"\nA = f\"TSTA{RUN[:4]}\"\nB = f\"TSTB{RUN[:4]}\"\n\nfailures = []\npasses = 0\n\n\ndef check(name, got, want):\n    global passes\n    if got == want:\n        passes += 1\n        print(f\"  PASS  {name}\")\n    else:\n        failures.append(f\"{name}: got {got!r}, want {want!r}\")\n        print(f\"  FAIL  {name}: got {got!r}, want {want!r}\")\n\n\nclass Ex:\n    \"\"\"Minimal order-entry client for the tests.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, nc, sender):\n        self.nc, self.sender = nc, sender\n        self.n = 0\n\n    def oid(self):\n        self.n += 1\n        return f\"{self.sender[:2]}{self.n:06d}\"[:8]\n\n    async def req(self, body):\n        msg = await self.nc.request(f\"ex.req.{self.sender}\", body.encode(), timeout=2.0)\n        return msg.data.decode().split()\n\n    async def add(self, side, vol, px, typ=\"L\", oid=None):\n        oid = oid or self.oid()\n        parts = await self.req(f\"{self.sender} A {FEED} {oid} {side} {vol} {px} {typ}\")\n        return oid, parts\n\n    async def cancel(self, oid):\n        return await self.req(f\"{self.sender} C {FEED} {oid}\")\n\n\ndef code(parts):\n    \"\"\"'Y n' -> ('Y', n); 'N code text' -> ('N', code).\"\"\"\n    if len(parts) >= 2 and parts[1] == \"Y\":\n        return (\"Y\", int(parts[2]) if len(parts) > 2 else 0)\n    if len(parts) >= 3:\n        return (\"N\", int(parts[2]))\n    return (\"?\", -1)\n\n\nasync def main():\n    nc = await nats.connect(NATS_URL)\n    a, b = Ex(nc, A), Ex(nc, B)\n\n    # Where a fresh, quiet price sits: far from the sample market if it is running,\n    # but inside the price band (ref 600 +/- 5000).\n    px = 300\n\n    print(\"\\nF (fill-and-kill) semantics\")\n    # CHANGELOG v2.3 claims F is atomic (\"fills in full or rejects\"). It is not.\n    oid, r = await a.add(\"B\", 10, px)\n    check(\"resting bid accepted\", code(r), (\"Y\", 0))\n    _, r = await b.add(\"S\", 25, px, typ=\"F\")\n    check(\"F sell of 25 into a bid of 10 fills 10, not 0 or 25\", code(r), (\"Y\", 10))\n    _, r = await b.add(\"S\", 5, px, typ=\"F\")\n    check(\"F does not rest: nothing left to trade against\", code(r), (\"Y\", 0))\n\n    print(\"\\nOrder ids are consumed permanently\")\n    oid, r = await a.add(\"B\", 1, px - 1)\n    check(\"add accepted\", code(r), (\"Y\", 0))\n    check(\"cancel removes it\", code(await a.cancel(oid)), (\"Y\", 1))\n    _, r = await a.add(\"B\", 1, px - 1, oid=oid)\n    check(\"re-using a cancelled id is rejected 203\", code(r), (\"N\", 203))\n\n    print(\"\\n206 vs 305 -- both mean 'not resting', for different reasons\")\n    check(\"cancelling an id never used -> 206\",\n          code(await a.cancel(\"zzzzzzzz\")), (\"N\", 206))\n    check(\"cancelling an already-cancelled id -> 305\",\n          code(await a.cancel(oid)), (\"N\", 305))\n    filled, r = await a.add(\"B\", 4, px - 2)\n    await b.add(\"S\", 4, px - 2, typ=\"F\")\n    check(\"cancelling a filled id -> 305\", code(await a.cancel(filled)), (\"N\", 305))\n\n    print(\"\\nE goes to the resting side, T to the aggressor\")\n    seen = {\"E\": [], \"T\": []}\n\n    async def collect(who):\n        async def cb(msg):\n            f = msg.data.decode().split()\n            if len(f) >= 2 and f[1] in (\"E\", \"T\"):\n                seen[f[1]].append((who, f[2], f[3]))\n        return cb\n\n    await nc.subscribe(f\"ex.md.{FEED}.{A}\", cb=await collect(\"A\"))\n    await nc.subscribe(f\"ex.md.{FEED}.{B}\", cb=await collect(\"B\"))\n    await asyncio.sleep(0.3)\n    await a.add(\"B\", 6, px - 3)          # A rests\n    await b.add(\"S\", 6, px - 3, typ=\"F\")  # B crosses\n    await asyncio.sleep(0.8)\n\n    check(\"resting side receives E\", [w for w, _, _ in seen[\"E\"]], [\"A\"])\n    check(\"aggressor receives T\", [w for w, _, _ in seen[\"T\"]], [\"B\"])\n\n    print(\"\\nRejects we rely on classifying\")\n    _, r = await a.add(\"B\", 1, 999999)\n    check(\"price outside the band -> 302\", code(r), (\"N\", 302))\n    _, r = await a.add(\"B\", 0, px)\n    check(\"zero volume -> 100 malformed\", code(r), (\"N\", 100))\n    _, r = await a.add(\"X\", 1, px)\n    check(\"bad side -> 205\", code(r), (\"N\", 205))\n    r = await a.req(f\"{B} A {FEED} aaaaaaaa B 1 {px} L\")  # wrong sender for subject\n    check(\"sender not matching the subject -> 200\", code(r), (\"N\", 200))\n\n    # Leave the book as we found it.\n    for ex in (a, b):\n        for i in range(1, ex.n + 1):\n            await ex.req(f\"{ex.sender} C {FEED} {ex.sender[:2]}{i:06d}\"[:40])\n    await nc.drain()\n\n    print(f\"\\n{passes} passed, {len(failures)} failed\")\n    for f in failures:\n        print(f\"  - Desk exposure hit 55.\"\"\"\n        self.h.inflight = -25                      # a sell hedge is outstanding\n        self.feed(\"hedger\", self.me, f\"1 T {self.me}:bbbbbbbb O:aaaaaaaa 25 650 1 S\")\n        self.assertEqual(self.h.positions[\"hedger\"], -25)\n        self.assertEqual(self.h.inflight, 0, \"confirmed fill should clear in-flight\")\n\n    def test_inflight_with_opposite_hedges_outstanding(self):\n        self.h.inflight = 10                       # a buy hedge outstanding\n        self.feed(\"hedger\", self.me, f\"1 T {self.me}:bbbbbbbb O:aaaaaaaa 10 650 1 B\")\n        self.assertEqual(self.h.inflight, 0)\n        self.assertEqual(self.h.desk(), 10, \"position confirmed, no longer in flight\")\n\n    def test_pnl_never_values_a_live_position_at_zero(self):\n        self.h.positions[\"hedger\"] = 10\n        self.h.cash = -6000\n        self.h.best_bid, self.h.best_ask = 640, 660\n        self.assertEqual(self.h.pnl(), -6000 + 10 * 650)\n        self.h.best_bid = self.h.best_ask = None    # book gone\n        self.h.last_mark = 640.0\n        self.assertEqual(self.h.pnl(), -6000 + 10 * 640)\n        self.h.last_mark = None\n        self.assertIsNone(self.h.pnl(), \"unmarkable must be None, not cash\")\n\n\nclass TakerSignal(unittest.TestCase):\n    def setUp(self):\n        self.t = taker.Taker(nc=None)\n\n    def bbo(self, bid, ask):\n        return Msg(f\"1 {taker.FEED} {bid} 5 {ask} 5\")\n\n    def test_repeated_identical_tops_are_not_recorded(self):\n        \"\"\"The BBO feed republishes unchanged: 154 msgs/sec vs 24.8 real changes.\n        Recording all of them made TAKER_LAG span ~32ms of duplicates.\"\"\"\n        self.t.last_trade_at = time.monotonic()   # suppress trading\n        for _ in range(50):\n            run(self.t.on_bbo(self.bbo(600, 604)))\n        self.assertEqual(len(self.t.mids), 1, \"duplicates must not fill the window\")\n\n    def test_distinct_tops_are_recorded(self):\n        self.t.last_trade_at = time.monotonic()\n        for bid in range(600, 610):\n            run(self.t.on_bbo(self.bbo(bid, bid + 4)))\n        self.assertEqual(len(self.t.mids), self.t.mids.maxlen)\n\n    def test_cooldown_suppresses_a_burst(self):\n        sent = []\n\n        async def fake_take(side):\n            sent.append(side)\n            self.t.last_trade_at = time.monotonic()\n\n        self.t.take = fake_take\n        for bid in range(600, 700, 5):            # a strong sustained move\n            run(self.t.on_bbo(self.bbo(bid, bid + 4)))\n        self.assertLessEqual(len(sent), 1,\n                             \"one cooldown window must not produce a burst of orders\")\n\n    def test_sell_reduces_position(self):\n        \"\"\"Both branches of apply_fill used to be `qty`, booking sells as buys.\"\"\"\n        self.t.apply_fill(\"B\", 10, 600)\n        self.t.apply_fill(\"S\", 4, 610)\n        self.assertEqual(self.t.position, 6)\n        self.assertEqual(self.t.cash, -10 * 600 + 4 * 610)\n\n    def test_fills_come_from_the_exchange_feed(self):\n        run(self.t.on_md(Msg(f\"1 T {taker.SENDER}:aaaaaaaa O:bbbbbbbb 3 642 1 B\")))\n        self.assertEqual(self.t.position, 3)\n        self.assertEqual(self.t.cash, -3 * 642, \"booked at the traded price\")\n\n    def test_pnl_is_none_when_nothing_can_be_marked(self):\n        self.t.position = 5\n        self.assertIsNone(self.t.pnl())\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    unittest.main(verbosity=2)\n"

  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# The desk's test suite. Four layers, cheapest first:\n#\n#   1. Go unit tests      -- quoter pricing, marking, fill sides, token bucket\n#   2. Python unit tests  -- hedger desk accounting, taker signal handling\n#   3. Protocol tests     -- the exchange behaviours the code depends on, asserted\n#                            against a live exchange on an empty book\n#   4. End-to-end smoke   -- the full stack on the sample market, asserting the\n#                            desk trades, stays flat, and logs no rejects\n#\n#   tests/run_tests.sh              # everything (~2 minutes)\n#   tests/run_tests.sh unit         # layers 1-2 only, no Docker network needed\n#   tests/run_tests.sh -d 90        # longer smoke window\n#\n# Everything runs in containers: no Go toolchain, no nats-py, no venv on the host.\nset -uo pipefail\ncd \"$(dirname \"$0\")/..\"\n\nSMOKE_SECS=45\nONLY=\"\"\nwhile [ $# -gt 0 ]; do\n  case \"$1\" in\n    -d) SMOKE_SECS=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n    unit|protocol|smoke) ONLY=\"$1\"; shift ;;\n    *) echo \"usage: $0 [unit|protocol|smoke] [-d SECONDS]\" >&2; exit 2 ;;\n  esac\ndone\n\nDC=(docker compose --profile sim --profile strategy)\nfails=0\nsection() { printf '\\n\\033[1m== %s ==\\033[0m\\n' \"$*\"; }\nok()   { printf '  \\033[32mPASS\\033[0m  %s\\n' \"$*\"; }\nbad()  { printf '  \\033[31mFAIL\\033[0m  %s\\n' \"$*\"; fails=$((fails + 1)); }\n\nwant() { # want <description> <actual> <expected>\n  if [ \"$2\" = \"$3\" ]; then ok \"$1\"; else bad \"$1 (got '$2', want '$3')\"; fi\n}\n\nrun_unit() {\n  section \"1. Go unit tests (quoter)\"\n  if docker run --rm -v \"$PWD/strategy\":/w -w /w golang:1.23-alpine \\\n       sh -c 'go test ./... 2>&1'; then ok \"go test\"; else bad \"go test\"; fi\n\n  section \"2. Python unit tests (hedger, taker)\"\n  docker build -q -t desk-test-py ./hedger >/dev/null || { bad \"build test image\"; return; }\n  if docker run --rm -v \"$PWD\":/repo -w /repo desk-test-py \\\n       python3 tests/test_seats.py 2>&1 | tail -5; then ok \"seat unit tests\"\n  else bad \"seat unit tests\"; fi\n}\n\nrun_protocol() {\n  section \"3. Protocol assertions (live exchange, empty book)\"\n  docker compose up -d >/dev/null 2>&1\n  sleep 5\n  docker build -q -t desk-test-py ./hedger >/dev/null\n  if docker run --rm --network candidate_default -e NATS_URL=nats://nats:4222 \\\n       -v \"$PWD/tests\":/t -w /t desk-test-py python3 test_protocol.py; then\n    ok \"protocol assertions\"\n  else\n    bad \"protocol assertions -- an assumption the seats rely on no longer holds\"\n  fi\n}\n\nrun_smoke() {\n  section \"4. End-to-end smoke (${SMOKE_SECS}s on the sample market)\"\n  # A stale exchange silently kills the sample market (used-up order ids), and\n  # `down` without profiles leaves seats running, so always reset with profiles.\n  \"${DC[@]}\" down --remove-orphans >/dev/null 2>&1\n  \"${DC[@]}\" up -d --build --force-recreate >/dev/null 2>&1\n  sleep 12\n  sleep \"$SMOKE_SECS\"\n\n  local tmp; tmp=\"$(mktemp -d)\"\n  \"${DC[@]}\" logs --no-log-prefix --tail 2000 strategy > \"$tmp/q.log\" 2>&1\n  \"${DC[@]}\" logs --no-log-prefix --tail 2000 hedger   > \"$tmp/h.log\" 2>&1\n  \"${DC[@]}\" logs --no-log-prefix --tail 2000 taker    > \"$tmp/t.log\" 2>&1\n  \"${DC[@]}\" down --remove-orphans >/dev/null 2>&1\n\n  # The desk actually traded. A silent, flat, zero-fill run looks healthy and is\n  # the single most misleading failure mode here -- it is what a dead sample\n  # market looks like, and what the shipped taker looked like for its whole life.\n  local qf; qf=$(grep -c 'fill ' \"$tmp/q.log\")\n  if [ \"$qf\" -gt 0 ]; then ok \"quoter traded ($qf fills)\"; else bad \"quoter never traded\"; fi\n\n  if grep -q 'pos=' \"$tmp/t.log\"; then ok \"taker reporting\"; else bad \"taker silent\"; fi\n  if grep -q 'desk=' \"$tmp/h.log\"; then ok \"hedger reporting\"; else bad \"hedger silent\"; fi\n\n  # The quoter quoted rather than only taking: it should have rested prices.\n  if grep -qE 'bid=[0-9]+x[0-9]+' \"$tmp/q.log\"; then ok \"quoter rested two-sided prices\"\n  else bad \"quoter never had a resting quote\"; fi\n\n  # Risk: the desk stayed bounded. This is the headline claim of Job 2.\n  local maxdesk\n  maxdesk=$(grep -o 'desk=[-0-9]*' \"$tmp/h.log\" | cut -d= -f2 |\n            awk '{a=($1<0?-$1:$1); if(a>m)m=a} END{print m+0}')\n  if [ \"${maxdesk:-999}\" -le 30 ]; then ok \"desk exposure bounded (max |desk| = $maxdesk)\"\n  else bad \"desk exposure reached $maxdesk\"; fi\n\n  # No seat should be logging rejects or errors in a healthy run.\n  local errs; errs=$(cat \"$tmp\"/*.log | grep -ciE 'reject|error|failed')\n  want \"no rejects or errors from any seat\" \"$errs\" \"0\"\n\n  # PnL must never be reported as bare cash while holding a position.\n  local badpnl\n  badpnl=$(grep 'pnl=' \"$tmp/q.log\" | awk '\n    {for(i=1;i<=NF;i++){if($i~/^pos=/)p=substr($i,5); if($i~/^cash=/)c=substr($i,6); if($i~/^pnl=/)n=substr($i,5)}\n     if(p+0!=0 && n==c) bad++} END{print bad+0}')\n  want \"position always marked into PnL\" \"$badpnl\" \"0\"\n\n  rm -rf \"$tmp\"\n}\n\ncase \"$ONLY\" in\n  unit)     run_unit ;;\n  protocol) run_protocol ;;\n  smoke)    run_smoke ;;\n  *)        run_unit; run_protocol; run_smoke ;;\nesac\n\nprintf '\\n'\nif [ \"$fails\" -eq 0 ]; then\n  printf '\\033[32mall checks passed\\033[0m\\n'\nelse\n  printf '\\033[31m%d check(s) failed\\033[0m\\n' \"$fails\"\nfi\nexit $((fails > 0))\n"
}

Test suite is in and green — 47 checks across four layers, everything running in containers so there's no Go toolchain, `nats-py` or venv needed on the host.

```
tests/run_tests.sh          # everything, ~2 min
tests/run_tests.sh unit     # layers 1-2, seconds, no network
```

| layer | covers | count |
|---|---|---|
| Go unit | pricing, skew floor, sizing, vol window, marking, fill sides, token bucket, id uniqueness | 12 |
| Python unit | hedger desk accounting + in-flight arithmetic, taker dedupe/cooldown/fill booking | 13 |
| Protocol | exchange behaviours asserted against a live exchange on an empty book | 15 |
| Smoke | full stack on the sample market | 7 |

**The protocol layer is the one I'd keep if I could keep only one.** Every assertion in it was found by experiment and contradicts or is missing from the shipped docs — `F` fills partially (the changelog claims otherwise in two places), order ids are consumed permanently, `206` and `305` mean different things, `E` goes to the resting side and `T` to the aggressor. The seats' correctness rests entirely on those. If the grading exchange is a different build and one stops holding, a seat goes *silently* wrong rather than loudly broken — which is the failure mode that cost the most time in this project. A red protocol test names the dead assumption directly.

The smoke test deliberately asserts the desk **traded**, not just that it ran. A quiet, flat, zero-fill session looks perfectly healthy in logs and is precisely what both a dead sample market and the shipped taker looked like. It also re-checks the two bugs whose symptom was plausible-looking output: desk exposure staying bounded, and PnL never reported as bare cash while holding a position.

**And I checked the suite can fail** — reintroducing `signed = qty if side == "B" else qty` turns `test_sell_reduces_position` red; reverting turns it green. A suite that has never failed isn't evidence of anything.

Two housekeeping notes: `strategy/go.sum` now exists (created by `go mod tidy` when running tests) and should be committed — it pins dependency hashes. And an untracked `.vscode/` has appeared, which you'll probably want to exclude from the submission tarball.

USER  2026-08-15 04:50:06

is there anything left to optimise?

==============================================================================
ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 04:50:42
==============================================================================
A few things, and one I'd rank well above the rest.

**1. The skew is now nearly inert — I introduced this when I made the edge adaptive.** `SkewTicks=4` was chosen when the edge was a fixed 2, so inventory leaning dominated the quote. With the adaptive edge, median volatility of 7.5 × multiplier 4.0 gives an edge around 30 — so at *maximum* inventory the quotes now shift by 4 against a half-spread of 30. That's a 13% lean where it used to be 200%. The quoter has effectively stopped managing its own inventory, which pushes all the flattening onto the hedger — the expensive route, and precisely the imbalance I complained about earlier. Skew should scale with the edge (`SkewTicks` as a *fraction* of edge, not an absolute tick count). Cheap to fix, principled, and testable.

**2. Clip and MaxPos have never been swept.** `Clip=5`, `MaxPos=25` are numbers I made up on day one. Clip scales both spread captured and adverse selection taken, so it's a genuine unknown, and unlike edge there's no theory telling me the right value.

**3. `VolWindow=2s` is unswept** — the horizon over which volatility is measured sets how fast the edge reacts to a regime change. 2 seconds was a guess.

**4. Statistical power is the real limiter.** Every conclusion in the notes rests on one or two runs of 60–120s, with within-setting spread of ~1,300–8,300. I can't currently resolve a difference smaller than a few thousand, which is the size of the effects I'd be chasing in (1)–(3). More repeats would make the tuning meaningful rather than anecdotal.

**What I'd leave alone:** the taker's thresholds and any further edge constants — both would be fitting to a market the task says isn't the graded one. And multi-instrument quoting (`AAM6`, `AAU6` are listed) is scope expansion, not optimisation; the hedger watches one feed and would need reworking.

**One thing that isn't optimisation but is a bigger risk than any of it:** we've never verified from a *clean checkout*. TASK.md requires `./run.sh --sim --strategy` to work from a fresh clone, and everything we've run has been in this working directory with images already built and `go.sum` generated by test runs. That's worth ten minutes before any more tuning.

My order: clean-checkout verification, then the skew fix (it's a real defect, not a preference), then a clip sweep with proper repeats if time allows. Want me to start with the checkout verification?

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 04:54:55
==============================================================================
Clean-checkout verification **passed**, and it earned its keep.

I tested it the way a grader actually sees it: extracted the pristine original repo (`git archive 657d59d`), overlaid *only* the deliverables — `strategy/ hedger/ taker/ tools/ tests/ NOTES.md TRANSCRIPT.txt docker-compose.yml CHANGELOG.md` — and ran in a differently-named directory. All three seats came up and traded, `desk=0`, **zero** error, reject or missing-file lines. So the deliverable list is complete; nothing the desk needs is stranded in your working directory.

**It caught one real bug.** `tests/run_tests.sh` hardcoded the Docker network as `candidate_default`, but Compose names the network after the *directory* — so the protocol tests could never have run from a grader's copy. Now derived from the running nats container. Nothing in the working directory could have revealed that.

**And it surfaced something more interesting.** I measured the quoter's actual quoted spread during that run:

```
samples 89 | min 4 | median 78 | mean 99 | max 240
```

against a market spread of 1–5 ticks. I'd estimated ~30 per side from `4.0 × median volatility 7.5` — the realised median is 78 *total*, and that max of 240 is exactly twice `MaxEdgeTicks=120`, so **the cap is binding regularly.**

The cause is a sampling effect I didn't think through: volatility is measured at the moments we requote, and we requote *when the market moves*. So the edge is priced off conditional volatility, not the unconditional median I calibrated against. The quoter has become liquidity of last resort — 32 fills in 90 seconds, trading only into large sweeps.

That's profitable here and defensible in a jumpy market, but it isn't what I set out to build, and the parameters were fitted against the wrong statistic. This now displaces my earlier list: `EdgeVolMult` and `MaxEdgeTicks` should be re-swept against *measured quoted spread* rather than inferred, and that interacts directly with the inert-skew problem, since skew of 4 against a half-spread of 39 is doing nothing at all.

Two housekeeping items before submission: `.vscode/` should be excluded from the tarball, and `strategy/go.sum` plus `tests/` are new files that need to be in it — they're in your working tree, so a tarball is fine, but building the archive from git alone would drop them.

Fill rate is the other half of the picture\n# -- a spread nobody crosses earns nothing.\nspread=\"$(grep -oE 'bid=[0-9]+x[0-9]+ ask=[0-9]+x[0-9]+' \"$tmp/q.log\" |\n  sed -E 's/bid=([0-9]+)x[0-9]+ ask=([0-9]+)x[0-9]+/\\1 \\2/' | awk '\n  {w=$2-$1; n++; s+=w; if(w>mx)mx=w; a[n]=w}\n  END{ if(!n){print \"- - -\"; exit}\n       for(i=1;i<=n;i++)for(j=i+1;j<=n;j++)if(a[j]<a[i]){t=a[i];a[i]=a[j];a[j]=t}\n       printf \"%d %.0f %d\", a[int(n/2)+1], s/n, mx }')\"\nread -r spmed spmean spmax <<<\"$spread\"\nqfills=\"$(grep -o 'fills=[0-9]*' \"$tmp/q.log\" | tail -1 | cut -d= -f2)\"\n\n# PnL: each seat marks its own inventory; \"n/a\" means it could not be valued.\npnl_of() { grep -o 'pnl=[-0-9]*' \"$1\" | tail -1 | cut -d= -f2; }"
}

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 05:09:06

Noisy and non-monotonic — `cap=40` gives −5,256 while its neighbours give +2,071, +255, +880. Also worth noting my new metric has a flaw: `cap=20` shows a *max* spread of 109 when 2×20=40 is the theoretical ceiling. The two resting quotes are repriced independently, so during a fast move the displayed pair comes from two different fair values. The median is meaningful; the max conflates edge with reprice lag.

Let me repeat the extremes rather than read a story into single runs:

More informative. `cap=20` is **consistent** (+2,071, +1,213 — range 858) while `cap=120` is wildly variable (+880, −4,434 — range 5,314).

But that raises a question about my own design: median volatility is 7.5, so `4.0 × 7.5 = 30` exceeds a cap of 20 most of the time — meaning at `cap=20` the adaptive edge is *pinned to the cap* and behaving like a fixed edge. Let me test that directly:

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 05:19:37
==============================================================================
Clean separation, and it answers the design question:

| config | quoter PnL | median spread |
|---|---|---|
| **adaptive, cap 20** | **+2,071, +1,213** | 33, 40 |
| fixed edge 20 | −2,530, −3,142 | 40, 40 |

Both runs of each land on the same side, and the ~4,500 gap exceeds the within-config spread (858, 612). **The adaptivity earns its place** — it tightens below the cap when the market is calm and pins to the cap when it's wild, where fixed-20 always quotes 20.

But `MaxEdgeTicks=20` is an absolute price constant — exactly the kind of thing I refused to hardcode for the edge itself. Making it relative to the price level so it transfers:

==============================================================================

  "new_string": "// edge returns the half-spread to quote, in price units. Caller must hold q.mu.\n//\n// The cap matters more than it sounds. Volatility is sampled at the moments we\n// requote, and we requote when the market moves, so the edge is priced off\n// *conditional* volatility and runs much wider than the unconditional average\n// suggests -- measured median quoted spread of 78 against an expected ~60, with\n// the cap binding regularly. Capping at ~20 measured better than leaving it loose\n// (+2,071/+1,213 against +880/-4,434) and far more consistently.\n//\n// It is expressed as a fraction of the price rather than an absolute, so it\n// transfers to an instrument trading at a different level -- the same reason the\n// edge itself is a volatility multiple rather than a tick count.\nfunc (q *quoter) edge() float64 {\n\te := q.cfg.EdgeTicks // floor: never quote tighter than this\n\tif q.cfg.EdgeVolMult > 0 {\n\t\tif v := q.cfg.EdgeVolMult * q.volatility(); v > e {\n\t\t\te = v\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif q.cfg.MaxEdgeFrac > 0 && q.haveMark && q.lastMark > 0 {\n\t\tif cap := q.cfg.MaxEdgeFrac * q.lastMark; e > cap {\n\t\t\te = cap\n\t\t}\n\t}\n\tif q.cfg.MaxEdgeTicks > 0 && e > q.cfg.MaxEdgeTicks {\n\t\te = q.cfg.MaxEdgeTicks // optional absolute backstop; off by default\n\t}\n\treturn e\n}"
}

| absolute cap 20 | **+2,071, +1,213** | 33, 40 | 62, 53 |
| fractional cap 0.033 (≈20 at mid 600) | **−2,834, −3,404** | 36, 18 | 71, 84 |

Non-overlapping pairs again, and the mechanism is visible: the fractional cap tightens when the price is *low* (0.033 × 440 = 14.5), so it quotes tighter and trades more into exactly the moves it should be avoiding. **This market's moves are absolute, not proportional** — `sim/market.py`'s mover steps 20–60 points regardless of price level — so scaling the cap with price is backwards here.

Reverting the default to what measured best, keeping the fraction available for a market where risk genuinely scales with price:

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 05:30:45
==============================================================================
Done, and the honest answer is less satisfying than the one I gave you two messages ago.

**The harness now measures the spread** rather than inferring it — `tools/bench.sh` reports median/mean/max quoted spread and quoter fill count per run.

**The sweep, quoter PnL at 120s:**

| config | runs |
|---|---|
| cap 20 | +2,071, +1,213, **−4,736** |
| cap 40 | −5,256 |
| cap 80 | +255 |
| cap 120 | +880, −4,434 |
| fixed edge 20 | −2,530, −3,142 |
| cap = 3.3% of price | −2,834, −3,404 |

**I have to withdraw what I told you earlier in this conversation.** After two runs each, the pairs looked cleanly separated and I wrote up "the adaptivity earns its place" and "follow the measurement, not the aesthetic" as established results. The third run of `cap 20` came back at −4,736 — so the within-config spread is ~6,800, which is **as large as every between-config difference I'd been interpreting**. Two runs landing the same side of zero is close to a coin flip landing twice; I read it as signal because it matched a mechanism I found plausible.

So these are not established, and I'm retracting them: that adaptive-capped-20 beats fixed-20; that the fractional cap is worse than the absolute one; and any ranking among the wide-edge settings. The *reasoning* about absolute-vs-proportional moves is still sound — this sim's mover does step 20–60 points regardless of price — but the data doesn't support the conclusion I drew from it.

**What survives** is the large effect, repeated across many runs: an edge sized to volatility beats the shipped fixed edge of 2 by roughly an order of magnitude. And risk control held in *every* run of the sweep — max desk ≤12, mean ≤2 — which is the one thing measured consistently enough to state plainly.

**Shipping `QUOTER_MAX_EDGE=20`** — not as proven-best, but because it sits inside the plausible band and bounds the pathological case: uncapped, the realised spread hit 240 against a market spread of 1–5, which is a quoter that has effectively left the market.

Doing this properly needs ~10 repeats per setting or 10-minute runs, ideally with the taker isolated since it supplies most of the variance — hours of wall time. I'd rather ship a defensible default with the uncertainty recorded than a tuned-looking number a fourth run would overturn. Notes updated with the retraction; tests still green.

ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 05:34:57
==============================================================================
Three things left that I think are worth doing, and one I'd stop doing.

**Stop:** parameter tuning. After the noise finding, any further sweep at this run length produces numbers that a fourth run overturns. That's spending hours to generate false confidence.

**1. Fix the inert skew — the last real design defect.** `QUOTER_SKEW=4` was chosen when the edge was a fixed 2, so at max inventory the quotes leaned 4 against a half-spread of 2 — a 200% lean. With the edge now capped at 20, that same 4 is a 20% lean. The quoter has largely stopped managing its own inventory, which pushes flattening onto the hedger, and the hedger pays the spread to do what skew does for free. Making skew a *fraction of the current edge* rather than an absolute tick count restores the intended behaviour at any edge width.

Crucially, this is verifiable **by unit test** — "at max position the lean is X% of the edge" is a deterministic property — so it doesn't need me to resolve the PnL noise. That's why I'd do it and wouldn't do another sweep.

**2. `NOTES.md` is 786 lines and TASK.md asks for "a short notes file."** That's a real mismatch with the spec. The detail is worth keeping — it's the evidence trail they asked for — but it needs a summary at the top: the design decisions, the assumptions, and the handful of findings that actually drive the code, in a page. A grader shouldn't have to read 786 lines to learn that `F` fills partially and the taker had never traded.

**3. Packaging.** `.vscode/` and `__pycache__/` are in the tree and shouldn't ship (the pycache is my test runner's fault — worth setting `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in it). `strategy/go.sum` and `tests/` need to be *in* the tarball. Then a final full test run, a transcript refresh, and the archive named `<your_email>.tar.gz` with the structure TASK.md specifies.

I'd do them in that order — the skew fix is the only one that changes how the desk behaves, and it should land before the final verification run so everything downstream tests the shipped code.

Want me to start with the skew?

}

  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "### Tooling written along the way",
  "new_string": "### Inventory skew: fixed the parameter, and it changed nothing\n\n`QUOTER_SKEW` was an absolute 4 ticks, chosen when the edge was a fixed 2 — a 200%\nlean at full inventory. Once the edge became volatility-sized and capped at 20, the\nsame 4 was a 20% lean, so the parameter silently stopped meaning what it said.\nReplaced with `QUOTER_SKEW_FRAC`, a fraction of the *current* edge, default 1.0:\nat full inventory the flattening side is pulled a whole edge toward fair and then\nheld off it by `MinEdgeTicks`. Unit-tested as a property (the lean scales 10x when\nthe edge scales 10x), so it does not depend on resolving PnL noise.\n\n**Then I measured whether it actually controls inventory, and it does not.** Added\nthe quoter's own position distribution to the harness — a distribution over the\nrun, far less noisy than a path-dependent PnL sum:\n\n| SkewFrac | mean abs quoter position | max |\n|---|---|---|\n| 1.0 (a full-edge lean) | 8.67 | 25 |\n| 0.2 (roughly the old inert lean) | 8.85 | 25 |\n\nIndistinguishable, and both run to the position limit.\n\n**Why, and it should have been obvious earlier:** skew works by making one side\nmore attractive than the other to a counterparty who is *choosing* between them.\nThe dominant flow here is the mover sweeping the book — it takes everything in its\npath up to a target price, and does not care that our bid is more attractive than\nour ask. You cannot lean your way out of being run over. Inventory here is imposed,\nnot chosen.\n\nThat is the real reason the hedger has to exist and has to be prompt, and it\nretires the theory I had been carrying since the first quoter: that the quoter\nshould flatten itself cheaply via skew and the hedger should only catch the tail.\nIn a sweep-driven market the quoter *cannot* flatten itself, whatever the lean.\n\n**Keeping the change** — the parameter now means what it says at any edge width,\nwhich is a correctness fix, and skew does work in a market with two-sided\nprice-sensitive flow. But it buys nothing measurable here and I am not claiming it\ndoes.\n\n### Tooling written along the way"

==============================================================================
ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 05:45:50
==============================================================================
Skew fix is in, unit-tested — and it produced a clean negative result that's more useful than the fix itself.

**The change:** `QUOTER_SKEW` (absolute 4 ticks) → `QUOTER_SKEW_FRAC` (fraction of the current edge, default 1.0). At full inventory the flattening side now gets pulled a whole edge toward fair and held off it by `MinEdgeTicks`. Tested as a *property* — the lean scales 10× when the edge scales 10× — so it doesn't depend on resolving the PnL noise.

**Then I measured whether it controls inventory. It doesn't.** Mean absolute quoter position: **8.67** at a full-edge lean, **8.85** at roughly the old inert lean. Both run to the `MaxPos=25` limit.

**And the reason should have been obvious to me much earlier.** Skew works by making one side more attractive than the other *to a counterparty who is choosing between them*. The dominant flow here is the mover sweeping the book — it takes everything in its path up to a target price and does not care which side of ours is more attractive. You can't lean your way out of being run over. **Inventory here is imposed, not chosen.**

That retires a theory I'd been carrying since the first version of the quoter: that the quoter should flatten itself cheaply via skew while the hedger catches only the tail. In a sweep-driven market the quoter *cannot* flatten itself at any lean — which is precisely why the hedger has to exist and has to be prompt.

I'm keeping the change, because the parameter now means what it says at any edge width and skew does work in markets with two-sided price-sensitive flow. But I'm not claiming it buys anything here.

That was the last item on the list that changes how the desk behaves. What remains is packaging: the `NOTES.md` summary (it's 786 lines against a brief asking for "short"), excluding `.vscode/`, and a final full test run plus transcript refresh before the tarball.

  "file_path": "/Users/emilygibbons/candidate/tools/run_summary.sh",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bash\n# Write a summary of how a desk run went, to runs/run-<timestamp>.md.\n#\n#   tools/run_summary.sh                 # pull logs from the compose stack\n#   tools/run_summary.sh --logs DIR      # use q.log / h.log / t.log in DIR\n#   tools/run_summary.sh -o report.md\n#\n# `run.sh` calls this on exit, so every run leaves a record without anyone\n# remembering to look at the terminal.\n#\n# The WARNINGS section matters more than the numbers. Several failure modes here\n# produce output that looks perfectly healthy: a dead sample market and a seat\n# that cannot place orders both show a quiet, flat, zero-fill log, and a position\n# valued at zero reads as profit. Those are checked explicitly.\nset -uo pipefail\ncd \"$(dirname \"$0\")/..\"\n\nOUT=\"\"\nLOGDIR=\"\"\nwhile [ $# -gt 0 ]; do\n  case \"$1\" in\n    -o) OUT=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n    --logs) LOGDIR=\"$2\"; shift 2 ;;\n    *) echo \"usage: $0 [-o FILE] [--logs DIR]\" >&2; exit 2 ;;\n  esac\ndone\n\ntmp=\"\"\nif [ -z \"$LOGDIR\" ]; then\n  tmp=\"$(mktemp -d)\"; LOGDIR=\"$tmp\"\n  DC=(docker compose --profile sim --profile strategy)\n  \"${DC[@]}\" logs --no-log-prefix --tail 5000 strategy > \"$LOGDIR/q.log\" 2>/dev/null\n  \"${DC[@]}\" logs --no-log-prefix --tail 5000 hedger   > \"$LOGDIR/h.log\" 2>/dev/null\n  \"${DC[@]}\" logs --no-log-prefix --tail 5000 taker    > \"$LOGDIR/t.log\" 2>/dev/null\nfi\ncleanup() { [ -n \"$tmp\" ] && rm -rf \"$tmp\"; }\ntrap cleanup EXIT\n\nQ=\"$LOGDIR/q.log\"; H=\"$LOGDIR/h.log\"; T=\"$LOGDIR/t.log\"\nfor f in \"$Q\" \"$H\" \"$T\"; do [ -f \"$f\" ] || : > \"$f\"; done\nif [ ! -s \"$Q\" ] && [ ! -s \"$H\" ] && [ ! -s \"$T\" ]; then\n  echo \"run_summary: no seat logs found (was the stack up?)\" >&2\n  exit 1\nfi\n\nmkdir -p runs\n[ -n \"$OUT\" ] || OUT=\"runs/run-$(date +%Y%m%d-%H%M%S).md\"\n\n# --- metrics ------------------------------------------------------------- #\nlast_field() { grep -o \"$2=[-0-9]*\" \"$1\" | tail -1 | cut -d= -f2; }\n\nqpnl=$(last_field \"$Q\" pnl);  qpos=$(last_field \"$Q\" pos);  qfills=$(last_field \"$Q\" fills)\ntpnl=$(last_field \"$T\" pnl);  tpos=$(last_field \"$T\" pos);  tfills=$(last_field \"$T\" fills)\nhpnl=$(last_field \"$H\" pnl);  hpos=$(grep -o 'hedger=[-0-9]*' \"$H\" | tail -1 | cut -d= -f2)\nhedges=$(last_field \"$H\" hedges); hlots=$(last_field \"$H\" traded)\ntotal=$(( ${qpnl:-0} + ${tpnl:-0} + ${hpnl:-0} ))\n\nread -r dmax dmean dover <<<\"$(grep -o 'desk=[-0-9]*' \"$H\" | cut -d= -f2 | awk '\n  {n++; a=($1<0?-$1:$1); s+=a; if(a>mx)mx=a; if(a>=25)o++}\n  END{ if(!n){print \"- - -\"; exit} printf \"%d %.2f %.0f\", mx, s/n, 100*o/n }')\"\n\nread -r spmed spmax <<<\"$(grep -oE 'bid=[0-9]+x[0-9]+ ask=[0-9]+x[0-9]+' \"$Q\" |\n  sed -E 's/bid=([0-9]+)x[0-9]+ ask=([0-9]+)x[0-9]+/\\1 \\2/' | awk '\n  {w=$2-$1; n++; a[n]=w; if(w>mx)mx=w}\n  END{ if(!n){print \"- -\"; exit}\n       for(i=1;i<=n;i++)for(j=i+1;j<=n;j++)if(a[j]<a[i]){t=a[i];a[i]=a[j];a[j]=t}\n       printf \"%d %d\", a[int(n/2)+1], mx }')\"\n\nread -r qposmean qposmax <<<\"$(grep -o 'pos=[-0-9]*' \"$Q\" | cut -d= -f2 | awk '\n  {n++; a=($1<0?-$1:$1); s+=a; if(a>mx)mx=a}\n  END{ if(!n){print \"- -\"; exit} printf \"%.2f %d\", s/n, mx }')\"\n\nerrs=$(cat \"$Q\" \"$H\" \"$T\" | grep -ciE 'reject|error|failed')\nfirst_ts=$(grep -oE '[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}' \"$Q\" | head -1)\nlast_ts=$(grep -oE '[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}:[0-9]{2}' \"$Q\" | tail -1)\n\n# --- report -------------------------------------------------------------- #\n{\n  echo \"# Desk run — $(date '+%Y-%m-%d %H:%M:%S')\"\n  echo\n  echo \"Window: ${first_ts:-?} to ${last_ts:-?} (seat clocks).\"\n  echo\n  echo '## Seats'\n  echo\n  echo '| seat | position | PnL | fills |'\n  echo '|---|---|---|---|'\n  printf '| quoter | %s | %s | %s |\\n' \"${qpos:--}\" \"${qpnl:--}\" \"${qfills:--}\"\n  printf '| taker  | %s | %s | %s |\\n' \"${tpos:--}\" \"${tpnl:--}\" \"${tfills:--}\"\n  printf '| hedger | %s | %s | %s hedges, %s lots |\\n' \\\n     \"${hpos:--}\" \"${hpnl:--}\" \"${hedges:--}\" \"${hlots:--}\"\n  printf '| **desk** | | **%s** | |\\n' \"$total\"\n  echo\n  echo '## Risk'\n  echo\n  printf -- '- Desk exposure: max |desk| **%s**, mean **%s**, %s%% of samples at/over 25\\n' \\\n     \"${dmax:--}\" \"${dmean:--}\" \"${dover:-0}\"\n  printf -- '- Quoter inventory: mean |pos| %s, max %s\\n' \"${qposmean:--}\" \"${qposmax:--}\"\n  echo\n  echo '## Quoting'\n  echo\n  printf -- '- Quoted spread: median %s, max %s\\n' \"${spmed:--}\" \"${spmax:--}\"\n  printf -- '- Rejects/errors logged across all seats: %s\\n' \"$errs\"\n  echo\n  echo '## Warnings'\n  echo\n  warned=0\n  note() { echo \"- $*\"; warned=1; }\n  [ \"${qfills:-0}\" -eq 0 ] 2>/dev/null && \\\n    note '**Quoter never traded.** A quiet zero-fill run is what a dead sample market and a seat that cannot place orders both look like. ASSISTANT  2026-08-15 05:54:56
==============================================================================
Found it — and it's a genuine bug in three places. `awk` variables persist across records, and the quoter's `fill` lines contain `pos=` but no `cash=`/`pnl=`. So a fill line inherits the *previous* status line's values — and when that previous line had `pos=0`, PnL legitimately equals cash. Every run would raise a false alarm.

Fixing it everywhere the pattern appears:

==============================================================================

  "file_path": "/Users/emilygibbons/candidate/NOTES.md",
  "old_string": "# Notes\n\n## Session 1 — probing the exchange before writing anything",
  "new_string": "# Notes\n\n## Summary\n\n**What I built.** A quoter (`strategy/`, Go) that rests two-sided liquidity with an\nedge sized from measured volatility, and a hedger (`hedger/`, Python) that keeps the\ndesk's combined position near zero by crossing the spread. The provided taker is\nrepaired but deliberately not retuned. Tests, a benchmark harness and a run\nsummariser are in `tests/` and `tools/`.\n\n**The finding that shaped everything.** The documentation is wrong in several\nplaces, and each error fails *silently* rather than loudly:\n\n| claim | reality |\n|---|---|\n| `F` orders fill in full or reject (CHANGELOG v2.3, repeated in `sim/market.py`) | they fill partially |\n| `T` duplicates `E` (implied by PROTOCOL) | `E` goes to the resting side, `T` to the aggressor; a seat needs both |\n| order ids are yours to choose | they are consumed permanently, even after a cancel |\n| `206`/`305` are interchangeable | different causes; both mean \"not resting\" |\n| the shipped taker works | it had never traded a single lot |\n\nSo the desk's rule is: **every seat derives its position from the exchange's own\nfill feed, never from what it asked for or what a sibling reports.** A hedger that\ntrusts its siblings' bookkeeping inherits their bugs.\n\n**Design decisions worth knowing.**\n\n- The quoter's edge is `EdgeVolMult * stdev(mid)`, not a tick count, so it adapts to\n  a market with different absolute moves. The multiplier is a risk appetite; the\n  resulting price is not a constant fitted to this sim.\n- The quoter never quotes through a minimum margin. Clearing inventory at a loss is\n  the hedger's job -- it can do it in one trade instead of waiting to be lifted.\n- Cancel by explicit order id, never `X` (cancel-many), which does not reliably\n  select by side and price.\n- Every seat rate-limits itself. Breaching `max_tps` *disconnects* the sender rather\n  than rejecting, which would end the session.\n\n**Results on the sample market.** Desk exposure held to a max of 4-12 lots and a\nmean near 1.5 while the seats carried 30+ gross -- Job 2 works, repeatably. On Job 1,\nsizing the edge to volatility is worth roughly an order of magnitude against the\noriginal fixed edge of 2. Absolute PnL is noisy enough that finer comparisons do not\nsurvive repetition (see the retraction below), and the taker is the dominant loss and\nthe dominant variance.\n\n**What I would not claim.** That any particular wide-edge setting is optimal; runs of\nidentical configuration differ by more than the effects being compared. Resolving\nthat needs ~10 repeats or 10-minute runs. I chose to record the uncertainty rather\nthan present a tuned-looking number a further run would overturn.\n\n**Known limitations.** One instrument only (`AAH6`). The taker still loses money on\nits own merits -- momentum in a market that mean-reverts inside a band -- and I left\nits parameters alone rather than fit them to a market the brief says is not the\ngraded one. The sample market deadlocks permanently if its book ever empties, which\nis a property of `sim/market.py`, not the desk.\n\n**How to read the rest of this file.** It is the working record, in the order things\nwere discovered, including the wrong turns and two retractions. Roughly: exchange\nprobing and the reject-code catalogue; what the sample market looks like; the\ntaker's bugs; why the first quoter lost money; the hedger; the three-seat result;\nthe test suite; the edge sweeps and the methodology correction; clean-checkout\nverification.\n\n## Session 1 — probing the exchange before writing anything"
